r\frac{\partial_{u}\phi_{c}}{\partial_{u}r}(u,0)=-k\quad\mbox{ and }\quad r\frac{\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi_{c}}{\partial_{\underline{u}}r}(u,0)=\frac{1}{k}.

(2.22)

Setting the scalar field function
\phi(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2})
used in this paper to be

\phi(u,\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=\sqrt{2}\phi_{c}(u,\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2}),

via
\partial_{u}r(u,0)=-1
we then translate (
2.22
) to (
2.21
).

Remark 13
.

At the end of this subsection, we point out that, along
\underline{u}=0
by employing equations

r\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\phi=-\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}\phi-\partial_{v}r\partial_{u}\phi
and
r\partial_{u}\partial_{v}r=-\partial_{u}r\partial_{v}r-\frac{1}{4}\Omega^{2}
, it holds

\frac{\partial}{\partial u}\frac{\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}-\frac{\Omega^{2}}{4r\partial_{v}r}\cdot\frac{\partial_{v}\phi}{\partial_{v}r}+\frac{\partial_{u}\phi}{r}=0.

(2.23)

Treat
\partial_{v}\phi(u,0)
as it is determined by other geometric quantities. Applying the fact

-\frac{\Omega^{2}}{4r\partial_{v}r}(u,0)=\frac{-1}{1-\mu^{*}}\cdot\frac{1}{r(u,0)}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\frac{\partial_{u}\phi}{r}(u,0)=\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}}\cdot\frac{1}{r(u,0)^{2}},

we can solve an ODE and we see that the below equality as stated in (
2.21
)

\frac{\partial_{v}\phi(u,0)}{\partial_{v}r(u,0)}=\frac{\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi(u,0)}{\partial_{\underline{u}}r(u,0)}=\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}}\cdot\frac{1}{r(u,0)}\approx\frac{1}{r(u,0)}

is debt to a special initial choice of
\partial_{v}\phi/\partial_{v}r
at
S_{-1,0}
. If we do not use this choice (by adding a general perturbation), back to (
2.23
), we would have that
\partial_{v}\phi(u,0)/\partial_{v}r(u,0)
behaviors like
r(u,0)^{\frac{-1}{1-\mu^{*}}}=r(u,0)^{-1}\cdot r(u,0)^{\frac{-\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}}
. With
\partial_{v}r(u,0)\approx r^{\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}}(u,0)
, it yields
\partial_{v}\phi(u,0)\approx r(u,0)^{-1}
. In below sections, we demonstrate an approach, which can deal with these general scenarios.

2.4.
Norms

For geometric quantities defined on
S_{u,\underline{u}}
, we now set norms that we will work with. We use
\psi\hskip 1.0pt
and
\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt
to denote the below geometric quantities

\psi\hskip 1.0pt\in\{\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi,\Omega\hat{\chi},\Omega\hat{\omega},\Omega\partial_{4}\phi\},

(2.24)

\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\in\{\eta,\underline{\eta},\,\Omega\underline{\omega}-\Omega\underline{\omega}(u,0),\,\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}},\,\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}(u,0),\nabla_{e_{1}}\phi,\,\nabla_{e_{2}}\phi,\,\Omega\partial_{3}\phi-\Omega\partial_{3}\phi(u,0)\}.

(2.25)

For conventional simplicity, we also write

\begin{split}\Omega\underline{\omega}(u,0)=&(\Omega\underline{\omega})_{0},\quad\Omega\partial_{3}\phi(u,0)=(\Omega\partial_{3}\phi)_{0},\\
\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}(u,0)=(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})_{0},&\quad\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi(u,0)=(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0},\quad\Omega(u,0)=\Omega_{0}.\end{split}

(2.26)

With constants
a,b
chosen as in Section
3
, we then define

\begin{split}\mathcal{O}_{i,\infty}(u,\underline{u}):=&\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{1}{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{i+2}\nabla^{i}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\mbox{ with }0\leq i\leq 2,\end{split}

(2.27)

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}_{i,\infty}(u,\underline{u}):=&\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{i+2}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\beta)\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{1}{\underline{u}a}\|u^{i+3}\nabla^{i}\rho\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
&+\frac{1}{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{i+3}\nabla^{i}\check{\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{1}{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{i+3}\nabla^{i}(K-\frac{1}{|u|^{2}})\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
&+\frac{1}{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{i+3}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\underline{\beta})\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\mbox{ with }0\leq i\leq 1.\end{split}

(2.28)

\begin{split}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(u,\underline{u}):=&\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{1}{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\mbox{ with }0\leq i\leq 4.\end{split}

(2.29)

We also set

\begin{split}\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(u,\underline{u}):=&\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{5}\nabla^{5}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi,\Omega\hat{\omega},\Omega\hat{\chi})\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
&+\bigg(\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}\bigg)^{-1}\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}\|u^{5}\nabla^{5}\underline{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{3}{2}}a^{\frac{3}{4}}}\|u^{6}\nabla^{5}\eta\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
&+\frac{\Omega}{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{5}\nabla^{5}\eta\|_{L^{2}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|\Omega u^{5}\nabla^{5}\eta\|_{L^{2}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
&+\bigg(1+\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}\bigg)^{-1}\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}\|u^{5}\nabla^{5}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}},\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\hat{\underline{\chi}})\|_{L^{2}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},\end{split}

(2.30)

\tilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{5,2}(u,\underline{u}):=\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{3}{2}}a}\|u^{6}\nabla^{5}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi)\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.

(2.31)

For
1\leq i\leq 4
, we further let

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}_{i,1}(u,\underline{u}):=&\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\beta_{A}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\nabla_{4}\phi\nabla_{A}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
&+\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|\Omega u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}(K-\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}-\frac{1}{4}\nabla^{A}\phi\nabla_{A}\phi,\check{\sigma})\|_{L^{2}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},\end{split}

(2.32)

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}_{i,2}(u,\underline{u}):=&\bigg(1+\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}\bigg)^{-1}\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{3}{2}}a^{\frac{3}{4}}}\|u^{i+2}\nabla^{i}(K-\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}-\frac{1}{4}\nabla^{A}\phi\nabla_{A}\phi,\check{\sigma})\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
&+\bigg(1+\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}\bigg)^{-1}\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{3}{2}}a^{\frac{3}{4}}}\|u^{i+2}\nabla^{i}(\underline{\beta}_{A}+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{3}\phi\nabla_{A}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.\end{split}

(2.33)

For
1\leq i\leq 5
, we then set

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{i,1}(u,\underline{u}):=&\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|\Omega u^{i}\nabla^{i}(e_{A}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},\end{split}

(2.34)

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{i,2}(u,\underline{u}):=&\bigg(1+\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}\bigg)^{-1}\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(e_{A}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
&+\bigg(1+\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}\bigg)^{-1}\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}\|\Omega^{-1}u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.\end{split}

(2.35)

And we further define
\mathcal{O}(u,\underline{u}),\,\mathcal{R}(u,\underline{u}),\,\mathcal{S}(u,\underline{u})
as

\mathcal{O}(u,\underline{u}):=\sum_{i\leq 2}\mathcal{O}_{i,\infty}(u,\underline{u})+\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(u,\underline{u}),\quad\mathcal{R}(u,\underline{u}):=\sum_{1\leq i\leq 4}\mathcal{R}_{i,1}(u,\underline{u})+\mathcal{R}_{i,2}(u,\underline{u}),

\mathcal{S}(u,\underline{u}):=\sum_{1\leq i\leq 4}\mathcal{S}_{i,1}(u,\underline{u})+\mathcal{S}_{i,2}(u,\underline{u}).

Next we denote
\mathcal{O}_{i,\infty},\,\mathcal{O}_{i,2},\,\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2},\,\tilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{5,2},\,\mathcal{R}_{i,\infty},\,\mathcal{R}_{i,1},\,\mathcal{R}_{i,2},\,\mathcal{S}_{i,1},\,\mathcal{S}_{i,2}
to be the supremum over
u,\underline{u}
in the considered spacetime region of
\mathcal{O}_{i,\infty}(u,\underline{u}),\,\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(u,\underline{u}),\,\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(u,\underline{u}),

\tilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{5,2}(u,\underline{u}),\,\mathcal{R}_{i,\infty}(u,\underline{u}),\mathcal{R}_{i,1}(u,\underline{u}),\,\mathcal{R}_{i,2}(u,\underline{u}),\,\mathcal{S}_{i,1}(u,\underline{u}),\,\mathcal{S}_{i,2}(u,\underline{u})
respectively. Finally, we set
\mathcal{O},\,\mathcal{R},\,\mathcal{S}
to be:

\mathcal{O}:=\sum_{i\leq 2}\mathcal{O}_{i,\infty}+\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2},\quad\mathcal{R}:=\sum_{1\leq i\leq 4}\mathcal{R}_{i,1}+\mathcal{R}_{i,2},\quad\mathcal{S}:=\sum_{1\leq i\leq 4}\mathcal{S}_{i,1}+\mathcal{S}_{i,2}.

We also denote
\mathcal{O}^{(0)},\,\tilde{\mathcal{O}}^{(0)},\,\tilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime(0)},\,\mathcal{R}^{(0)},\,\mathcal{S}^{(0)}
to be the corresponding norms along the initial hypersurfaces
u=-1
and
\underline{u}=0
. Lastly, we define an initial data quantity

\mathcal{I}^{(0)}:=\sup_{0\leq\underline{u}\leq\delta}\mathcal{I}^{(0)}(\underline{u})

with

\begin{split}\mathcal{I}^{(0)}(\underline{u}):=1+\sum_{0\leq m\leq 6,}\sum_{0\leq k\leq 5}\frac{1}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|(\nabla)^{m}(\nabla_{4})^{k}(\hat{\chi},\nabla_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{2}(S_{-1,\underline{u}})}.\end{split}

Remark 14
.

Following the arguments carried out in detail in Chapter of
[
21
]
, we have

\sum_{i\leq 2}\mathcal{O}_{i,\infty}(-1,\underline{u})+\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(-1,\underline{u})+\sum_{i\leq 2}\mathcal{R}_{i,\infty}(-1,\underline{u})\lesssim\mathcal{I}^{(0)}.

In Section
7
, we will employ the initial values of
\Omega\kappa,\,\underline{\mu},\,\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}
along
H_{-1}
, with

\kappa:=\nabla\hat{\omega}+{{}^{*}\nabla\omega^{\dagger}}-\frac{1}{2}(\beta-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{4}\phi\nabla\phi),\quad\underline{\mu}:=-\mbox{div }\underline{\eta}+K-\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}-\frac{1}{4}\nabla^{A}\phi\nabla_{A}\phi.

Note that we set
\omega^{\dagger}
to be
0
and
\Omega
to be
1
along
H_{-1}
. And it holds

\|u^{4}\nabla^{4}(\Omega\kappa)\|_{L^{2}(S_{-1,\underline{u}})}\lesssim a^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\|u^{5}\nabla^{4}\underline{\mu}\|_{L^{2}(S_{-1,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\|u^{5}\nabla^{5}(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})\|_{L^{2}(S_{-1,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}.

2.5.
Exterior and Interior Regions

To establish the hyperbolic existence result, we first choose a
u_{1}<0
satisfying
0<|u_{1}|\ll 1
and then divide the spacetime
\mathcal{M}
into two regions:

\cdot

the exterior:

-1\leq u\leq u_{1}
and
0\leq\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq|u_{1}|\Omega^{2-\tilde{\delta}}(u_{1},0)
,

\cdot

the interior:
u_{1}\leq u\leq 0
and
0\leq\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq|u|\Omega^{2-\tilde{\delta}}(u,0)
.

Remark 15
.

For notational simplicity, in the rest of this paper, we set constant
\tilde{\delta}
obeying
0<\tilde{\delta}\ll 1
. In reality, if needed we can set
\tilde{\delta}
to be any number satisfying
0<\tilde{\delta}<1/2
and all the below proof stays the same.

In this article, we will use the below inequality frequently

\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|\Omega}\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|\Omega^{\frac{3}{2}}}\ll 1.

(2.36)

This inequality is correct because (with
a=1
)

\cdot

in the exterior we have

\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|\Omega^{\frac{3}{2}}}\leq\frac{|u_{1}|\Omega^{2-\tilde{\delta}}(u_{1},0)}{|u|\Omega^{\frac{3}{2}}}=\frac{|u_{1}|\Omega^{\frac{3}{2}}(u_{1},0)}{|u|\Omega^{\frac{3}{2}}}\cdot\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}-\tilde{\delta}}(u_{1},0)\ll 1
,

\cdot

in the interior we have
\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|\Omega^{\frac{3}{2}}}\leq\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}-\tilde{\delta}}(u,0)\lesssim\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}-\tilde{\delta}}(u_{1},0)\ll 1
.

With above discussion, we can further choose a constant
B
, such that in both regions

\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|\Omega}\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|\Omega^{\frac{3}{2}}}\leq\frac{1}{B}\ll 1.

(2.37)

2.6.
An Approach of Bootstrap

In this article, we carry out a bootstrap argument to prove uniform upper bounds for
\mathcal{O},\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{S}
. For initial data along
\underline{u}=0
and
u=-1
, we have

\mathcal{O}^{(0)}+\mathcal{R}^{(0)}+\mathcal{S}^{(0)}\leq C_{0}\mathcal{I}^{(0)},

where
C_{0}
depends only on initial data. For notational simplicity, we rewrite the above line as

\mathcal{O}^{(0)}+\mathcal{R}^{(0)}+\mathcal{S}^{(0)}\lesssim\mathcal{I}^{(0)}.

For the rest of this article, we keep the same notation: if
P\leq C_{0}Q
with constant
C_{0}
depending only on the initial data, we could write it as

P\lesssim Q.

Under this notation, we have
\mathcal{I}^{(0)}\lesssim 1
.

Our goal in below sections is to show that in the considered spacetime region, it holds

\mathcal{O}+\mathcal{R}+\mathcal{S}\leq\mathcal{I}^{(0)}+(\mathcal{I}^{(0)})^{2}+1.

To achieve this, we make bootstrap assumptions

\mathcal{O}(u,\underline{u})\leq O,

(2.38)

\mathcal{R}(u,\underline{u})\leq R,

(2.39)

\mathcal{S}(u,\underline{u})\leq S,

(2.40)

\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(u,\underline{u})\leq\tilde{O},\quad\tilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{5,2}(u,\underline{u})\leq\tilde{O}^{\prime}.

(2.41)

Recall (
2.37
). For constants in bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
)-(
2.41
), for the rest of this paper, we assume that they satisfy

1\leq O,R,S,\tilde{O}\ll(\tilde{O}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{8}}\ll B^{\frac{1}{32}}\leq(\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|\Omega^{\frac{3}{2}}})^{-\frac{1}{32}}\leq(\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|\Omega})^{-\frac{1}{32}}.

(2.42)

In below, we demonstrate the steps for improving the bootstrap assumptions:
6
6

6

A more detailed explanation of the bootstrap method can be found in Section 3.1 of
[
4
]
.

In Section
4
we prove

\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\hat{\chi},\mbox{tr}\chi)\lesssim 1
with improved estimates for
\mbox{tr}\chi
,

\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\hat{\omega})\lesssim 1+\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\nabla_{4}\phi)
,

\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt)\lesssim 1+\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\hat{\omega})+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(\hat{\omega})+\mathcal{R}(\beta)
with improved bound for
\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{|u|}
.

In Section
5
we obtain

\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\nabla_{4}\phi)\lesssim 1,\quad\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\nabla_{A}\phi)\lesssim S,\quad\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\nabla_{3}\phi)\lesssim S
.

In Section
6
we derive

\mathcal{S}_{5,1}\lesssim 1
,
\mathcal{S}_{5,2}\lesssim 1
.

With the bounds obtained in Section
5
and Section
6
, we revisit the estimates in Section
4
. Hence we now get

\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\psi\hskip 1.0pt)\lesssim 1
,
\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt)\lesssim 1+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(\hat{\omega})+\mathcal{R}(\beta)
.

In Section
7
we show

\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(\hat{\omega})\lesssim 1+\mathcal{R}(\beta)
,
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(\mbox{tr}\chi,\hat{\chi})\lesssim 1+\mathcal{R}(\beta)
,

\tilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{5,2}(\mbox{tr}\chi)\lesssim 1
,

\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(\underline{\eta})\lesssim 1
,
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(\eta)\lesssim 1+\mathcal{R}(K,\check{\sigma})+\mathcal{R}(\beta)+\underline{\mathcal{R}}(K,\check{\sigma})
,

\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})\lesssim 1
,
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}})\lesssim 1
,
\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(\hat{\underline{\chi}})\lesssim\mathcal{R}(\beta)
.

In Section
8
we derive

\mathcal{R}(\beta)+\underline{\mathcal{R}}(K,\check{\sigma})\lesssim 1
,
\mathcal{R}(K,\check{\sigma})+\underline{\mathcal{R}}(\underline{\beta})\lesssim 1
.

Remark 16
.

The proof strategy and the main theorems of this paper also hold for perturbed Christodoulou’s initial data prescribed along
\underline{u}=0
. Denote

\mathring{\Omega}(u,0)=\Omega(u,0)\mbox{ and }\mathring{\Gamma}(u,0)\in\{\frac{\partial_{u}r}{r}(u,0),\,\frac{\partial_{\underline{u}}r}{r}(u,0),\,\partial_{u}\phi(u,0),\,\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi(u,0)\}.

We further set
\mathring{\Omega}_{c}(u,0)
and
\mathring{\Gamma}_{c}(u,0)
to be the corresponding values of Christodoulou’s naked-singularity solution along
\underline{u}=0
. Our below proof strategy also extends to spherically-symmetric initial data along
\underline{u}=0
satisfying

|\frac{\mathring{\Omega}(u,0)}{\mathring{\Omega}_{c}(u,0)}|\leq 1+\frac{\mathring{\Omega}_{c}(u,0)^{\tilde{\delta}}}{a^{\frac{1}{3}}},\quad|\frac{\mathring{\Gamma}(u,0)}{\mathring{\Gamma}_{c}(u,0)}|\leq 1+\frac{\mathring{\Omega}(u,0)^{\tilde{\delta}}}{a^{\frac{1}{3}}}

with constants
0<\tilde{\delta}\ll 1
and
a\geq 1
.
7
7

7

The reader is also referred to a spherically-symmetric extension of Christodoulou’s naked-singularity solution by Singh in
[
37
]
.
For simplicity of the proof and for more precise statements of the main theorems, in the main body of this paper we stick to Christodoulou’s naked-singularity initial data with Footnotes
4
,
9
,
13
,
14
,
15
,
16
,
17
and Remark
20
added to explain how to deal with the perturbed scenario.
8
8

8

The methods developed in this paper also allow non-spherically-symmetric initial data prescribed along
\underline{u}=0
, the author will provide the details in a separate paper.

3.
Preliminary Estimates

3.1.
Estimates for metric components

We now start the hyperbolic estimates. We first obtain a bound for
\Omega
under the bootstrap assumptions:

Proposition 3.1
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
), (
2.41
), we have

\|\frac{\Omega}{\Omega(u,0)}-1\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq B^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

We employ the equation

\Omega\hat{\omega}=-\frac{\Omega}{2}\nabla_{4}\log\Omega={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{\partial}{\partial\underline{u}}+d^{A}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta^{A}})\log\Omega.}

(3.1)

Integrating equation (
3.1
) along
e_{4}
direction, we obtain

||\log\frac{\Omega}{\Omega(u,0)}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\Omega\hat{\omega}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}d\underline{u}^{\prime}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}O}{|u|}.

Here we use the bootstrap assumption (
2.38
).
This implies that

\|\frac{\Omega}{\Omega(u,0)}-1\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}O}{|u|}\leq B^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

∎

We then control
g\mkern-9.0mu/
under the bootstrap assumptions. Following the same argument as for Proposition 5.2 in
[
2
]
, we have

Proposition 3.2
.

Fix a point
(u,\omega)
on the initial hypersurface
\underline{H}_{0}
. Along the outgoing characteristic emanating from
(u,\omega)
, we define
\Lambda(\underline{u})
and
\lambda(\underline{u})
to be the larger and smaller eigenvalue of
g\mkern-9.0mu/^{-1}(u,\underline{u}=0,\omega)g\mkern-9.0mu/(u,\underline{u},\omega)
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
) and (
2.41
), then it holds

|\Lambda(\underline{u})-1|+|\lambda(\underline{u})-1|\leq B^{-\frac{1}{2}}

for every
\underline{u}\in[0,\delta]
. Consequently, for every
\underline{u}\in[0,\delta]
, this also implies

|\xi(\underline{u})-1|\leq B^{-\frac{1}{2}}\quad\mbox{ with }\quad\xi(\underline{u})=\frac{dvol_{g\mkern-9.0mu/_{\underline{u}}}}{dvol_{g\mkern-9.0mu/_{0}}}.

As corollaries, we also obtain the below area estimate and the Sobolev embedding.

Proposition 3.3
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
), (
2.41
), it holds that

\sup_{u,\underline{u}}|\mbox{Area}(S_{u,\underline{u}})-\mbox{Area}(S_{u,0})|\leq|u|^{2}B^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Proposition 3.4
.

With the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
) and (
2.41
), it holds that

\begin{split}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim&\sum_{i\leq 2}\|u^{i-1}\nabla^{i}\phi\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.\end{split}

(3.2)

Note that the detailed proof of the Sobolev embedding is provided in Propositions 5.6-5.9 in
[
2
]
.

3.2.
Estimates for Transport Equations

In this article, we will employ the below two propositions frequently:

Proposition 3.5
.

With the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
) ,(
2.41
), for an
S_{u,\underline{u}}
-tangent tensor field
\phi
of arbitrary rank it holds

||\phi||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim||\phi||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}})}+\int_{\underline{u}^{\prime}}^{\underline{u}}||\Omega\nabla_{4}\phi||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime\prime}})}d{\underline{u}^{\prime\prime}}.

Proposition 3.6
.

Set
\phi
and
F
to be
S_{u,\underline{u}}
-tangent tensor fields of rank
k
obeying the below transport equation:

\Omega\nabla_{3}\phi_{A_{1}...A_{k}}+\lambda_{0}\Omega{\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}}\phi_{A_{1}...A_{k}}=\Omega F_{A_{1}...A_{k}}

and denote
\lambda_{1}=2(\lambda_{0}-\frac{1}{2})
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
), (
2.41
), then it holds that

|u|^{\lambda_{1}}\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(S_{-1,\underline{u}})}+\int_{-1}^{u}|u^{\prime}|^{\lambda_{1}}\|\Omega F\|_{L^{2}(S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}})}du^{\prime}.

The proof of the above two propositions are the same as in Proposition 5.4 and Proposition 5.5 of
[
2
]
and hence are omitted.
9
9

9

With perturbed Christodoulou initial data, the quantity
\|\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}(u,\underline{u},\omega)+\frac{2}{|u|}\|_{L^{1}_{u}}
is still uniformly bounded and hence the proof for Proposition
3.6
is the same.

3.3.
Commutation Formula

We quote the following formula from
[
27
]
:

Proposition 3.7
.

The commutator
[\nabla_{4},\nabla]
acting on an
(0,r)
S-tensor satisfies

\begin{split}&[\nabla_{4},\nabla_{B}]\phi_{A_{1}...A_{r}}\\
=&[D_{4},D_{B}]\phi_{A_{1}...A_{r}}+(\nabla_{B}\log\Omega)\nabla_{4}\phi_{A_{1}...A_{r}}-(g\mkern-9.0mu/^{-1})^{CD}\chi_{BD}\nabla_{C}\phi_{A_{1}...A_{r}}\\
&-\sum_{i=1}^{r}(g\mkern-9.0mu/^{-1})^{CD}\chi_{BD}\underline{\eta}_{A_{i}}\phi_{A_{1}...\hat{A_{i}}C...A_{r}}+\sum_{i=1}^{r}(g\mkern-9.0mu/^{-1})^{CD}\chi_{A_{i}B}\underline{\eta}_{D}\phi_{A_{1}...\hat{A_{i}}C...A_{r}}.\end{split}

Proposition 3.8
.

The commutator
[\nabla_{3},\nabla]
acting on an
(0,r)
S-tensor satisfies

\begin{split}&[\nabla_{3},\nabla_{B}]\phi_{A_{1}...A_{r}}\\
=&[D_{3},D_{B}]\phi_{A_{1}...A_{r}}+(\nabla_{B}\log\Omega)\nabla_{3}\phi_{A_{1}...A_{r}}-(g\mkern-9.0mu/^{-1})^{CD}\underline{\chi}_{BD}\nabla_{C}\phi_{A_{1}...A_{r}}\\
&-\sum_{i=1}^{r}(g\mkern-9.0mu/^{-1})^{CD}\underline{\chi}_{BD}\eta_{A_{i}}\phi_{A_{1}...\hat{A_{i}}C...A_{r}}+\sum_{i=1}^{r}(g\mkern-9.0mu/^{-1})^{CD}\underline{\chi}_{A_{i}B}\eta_{D}\phi_{A_{1}...\hat{A_{i}}C...A_{r}}.\end{split}

By induction, using commutation formula repeatedly we get the schematic form:

Proposition 3.9
.

Assume
\Omega\nabla_{4}\phi=\Omega F_{0}
. Let
\Omega\nabla_{4}\nabla^{i}\phi=\Omega F_{i}
.
Then it holds

\begin{split}\Omega F_{i}=&\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega F_{0})+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega\chi)\nabla^{i_{4}}\phi\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i-1}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega\beta)\nabla^{i_{4}}\phi.\end{split}

Here by
\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}
we mean the sum of all terms, which itself is a product of
i_{2}
factors, each factor being
\nabla^{j}(\eta+\underline{\eta})
for some
j\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
and that the sum of all
j
being
i_{1}
, i.e.,
\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}=\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1}+...+j_{i_{2}}=i_{1}}\nabla^{j_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})...\nabla^{j_{i_{2}}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})
. Similarly, assume
\Omega\nabla_{3}\phi=\Omega G_{0}
. Suppose
\Omega\nabla_{3}\nabla^{i}\phi=\Omega G_{i}
.
Then we have

\begin{split}\Omega G_{i}=-\frac{i}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\nabla^{i}\phi&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega G_{0})\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}},\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})_{0})\nabla^{i_{4}}\phi\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i-1}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega\underline{\beta})\nabla^{i_{4}}\phi.\end{split}

By replacing
\Omega\beta
and
\Omega\underline{\beta}
via the schematic Codazzi equations:

\Omega\beta=\nabla\psi\hskip 1.0pt+\psi\hskip 1.0pt\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt,\quad\Omega\underline{\beta}=\nabla\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt+\frac{1}{|u|}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt+\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\,\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt,

rewriting
\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi
,
\Omega\hat{\chi}
as
\psi\hskip 1.0pt
and substituting
\eta
,
\underline{\eta}
,
\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}
,
\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})_{0}
with
\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt
, we obtain a simplified version

Proposition 3.10
.

Suppose
\nabla_{4}\phi=F_{0}
. Let
\nabla_{4}\nabla^{i}\phi=F_{i}
.
Then we have

\begin{split}\Omega F_{i}=&\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega F_{0})+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\nabla^{i_{4}}\phi.\\
\end{split}

Similarly, assume
\nabla_{3}\phi=G_{0}
. Let
\nabla_{3}\nabla^{i}\phi=G_{i}
.
Then it holds

\begin{split}\Omega G_{i}+\frac{i}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\nabla^{i}\phi=&\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega G_{0})\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}\phi\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i,\\
i_{3}\leq i-1\end{subarray}}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\Omega\nabla_{3}\phi)\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}\phi.\end{split}

Remark 17
.

For the rest of this paper, when there is no danger of confusion, the constants on the left of the equations are kept precise. The uniform constants in front of coefficients on the right are usually omitted.

3.4.
Elliptic Estimates for Hodge Systems

We first recall the definition of the divergence and curl of a symmetric covariant tensor with an arbitrary rank:

(\mbox{div }\phi)_{A_{1}...A_{r}}:=\nabla^{B}\phi_{BA_{1}...A_{r}},

(\mbox{curl }\phi)_{A_{1}...A_{r}}:={\epsilon}\mkern-8.0mu/\,^{BC}\nabla_{B}\phi_{CA_{1}...A_{r}}.

Here
{\epsilon}\mkern-8.0mu/\,
is the volume form associated to the metric
g\mkern-9.0mu/
on
S_{u,\underline{u}}
.
Recall that the trace is defined via

(\mbox{tr }\phi)_{A_{1}...A_{r-1}}=(g\mkern-9.0mu/^{-1})^{BC}\phi_{BCA_{1}...A_{r-1}}.

In this article, we will use the following
L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})
elliptic estimates proved in
Chapter 7
of
[
21
]
.

Proposition 3.11
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
), (
2.41
), denoting
\phi
to be a totally symmetric
r+1
covariant tensorfield on a 2-sphere
(S_{u,\underline{u}},g\mkern-9.0mu/)
satisfying

\mbox{div }\phi=\tilde{f},\quad\mbox{curl }\phi=\tilde{g},\quad\mbox{tr}\phi=\tilde{h},

then, for
1\leq i\leq 5
, it holds that

\begin{split}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}\phi||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}(||u^{j+1}\nabla^{j}(\tilde{f},\tilde{g})||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\|u^{j}\nabla^{j}\tilde{h}\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+||u^{j}\nabla^{j}\phi||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}).\end{split}

As a special case with
\phi
being a symmetric traceless 2-tensor, it holds that
\mbox{curl }\phi=^{*}\mbox{div }\phi
. Using the above proposition, we then get

Proposition 3.12
.

Assume that
\phi
is a symmetric traceless 2-tensor obeying

\mbox{div }\phi=\tilde{f}.

Then, under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
), (
2.41
), for
1\leq i\leq 5
, it holds

\begin{split}||u^{i}\nabla^{i}\phi||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim&\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}(||u^{j+1}\nabla^{j}\tilde{f}||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+||u^{j}\nabla^{j}\phi||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}).\end{split}

4.
L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})
estimates for Ricci coefficients

We then proceed to derive the
L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})
estimates for the Ricci coefficients and their first four angular derivatives.

We first state a useful lemma, which can be verified directly.
10
10

10

A detailed proof is provided in Proposition 6.1 of
[
2
]
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
), (
2.41
), it holds that

\begin{split}\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}\leq 4}\|u^{i_{1}+i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim&\sum_{i_{1}\leq 4}\|u^{i_{1}+1}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}\leq 2}\|u^{i_{1}+i_{2}+2}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim&\sum_{i_{1}\leq 2}\|u^{i_{1}+2}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.\end{split}

In particular, by (
2.38
), we have

\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}\leq 4}\|u^{i_{1}+i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}\leq 2}\|u^{i_{1}+i_{2}+2}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}O.

We now start from the
L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})
estimates for
\Omega\hat{\chi}
.

Proposition 4.2
.

With the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
), (
2.41
), it holds

\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\hat{\chi})\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim a^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

We employ the null structure equation

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}\nabla_{3}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}\hat{\chi})+\frac{1}{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}\hat{\chi})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}\nabla\eta+\psi\hskip 1.0pt\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt+\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt.

After commuting this equation with
i
angular derivatives, we obtain

\begin{split}&\Omega\nabla_{3}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\hat{\chi})+\frac{i+1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\hat{\chi})\\
=&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}\nabla^{i+1}\eta+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+2}+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i-1}\frac{1}{|u|}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt.\end{split}

Note that the fourth term only presents when
i\geq 1
.

To bound
\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\hat{\chi})\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}L_{\underline{u}}^{\infty}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
, we then apply Proposition
3.6
with
\lambda_{0}=\frac{i+1}{2}
and estimate each term on the right.

For the
\nabla^{i+1}\eta
term, when
i=4
we have

\begin{split}\|u^{4}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}\nabla^{5}\eta\|_{L_{u}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq\|\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}}{|u|}\|_{L^{2}_{u}}\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}u^{5}\nabla^{5}\eta\|_{L_{u}^{2}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-\frac{1}{4}}}.\end{split}

The rest terms are lower order. For example, we control the last term as

\begin{split}&\sum_{i\leq 4}\|\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i-1}u^{i-1}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L_{u}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}aO^{2}}{|u|}\leq a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-\frac{1}{4}}.\end{split}

(4.1)

All the remaining terms on the right obey the same upper bound
a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-\frac{1}{4}}
.

For initial data, we have
\sum_{i\leq 4}\|\nabla^{i}(\Omega\hat{\chi})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{-1,\underline{u}})}\leq a^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Gathering all above estimates, we get

\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\hat{\chi})\|_{L^{\infty}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim a^{\frac{1}{2}}.

∎

We then derive the estimates for
\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi
and its derivatives. We will also point out that
\nabla^{i}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi)
obeys a better bound than the standard estimate for
\nabla^{i}\psi\hskip 1.0pt
.

Proposition 4.3
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
), (
2.41
), it holds

\displaystyle\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi-

\displaystyle\Omega^{2}(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0})\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\underline{u}aO^{2}\ll|u|a^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega

\displaystyle\mbox{tr}\chi)\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}aO^{2}}{|u|}\ll a^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

We use the following null structure equation

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}\nabla_{4}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{-1}}\mbox{tr}\chi)=-|\hat{\chi}|^{2}-(\nabla_{4}\phi)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}(\mbox{tr}\chi)^{2}.

Note that
\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi(u,0)=2(1-\mu^{*})/|u|
. Utilizing
\nabla_{4}(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0}=0
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}\nabla_{4}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{-1}}\mbox{tr}\chi-(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0})=-|\hat{\chi}|^{2}-(\nabla_{4}\phi)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}(\mbox{tr}\chi)^{2}.

Commuting this equation with
i
angular derivatives, we get the below form

\begin{split}&\Omega\nabla_{4}\nabla^{i}(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi-(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0})\\
=&\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\hat{\chi},\partial_{4}\phi)\nabla^{i_{4}}(\hat{\chi},\partial_{4}\phi)+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i}\Omega^{-1}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\nabla^{i_{4}}\mbox{tr}\chi\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}}{|u|}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt.\end{split}

We then employ Proposition
3.5
and control the right hand side in the
\|u^{i+1}\cdot\|_{L^{1}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
norm to bound
\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi-(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
.

Remark 18
.

In below estimates and for the rest of this article, when there is no danger of confusion, we use
\Omega^{-1}
to stand for
\|\Omega^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
.

Employing the bootstrap assumption (
2.38
) together with Sobolev embedding in Proposition
3.4
, for the case
i_{2}=0
, we first control

\begin{split}&\sum_{i\leq 4}\|\sum_{i_{3}+i_{4}=i}u^{i+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\hat{\chi},\partial_{4}\phi)\nabla^{i_{4}}(\hat{\chi},\partial_{4}\phi)\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
\lesssim&\underline{u}\sum_{i_{3}\leq 2}\|u^{i_{3}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\hat{\chi},\partial_{4}\phi)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\sum_{i_{4}\leq 4}\|u^{i_{4}}\nabla^{i_{4}}(\hat{\chi},\partial_{4}\phi)\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\Omega^{-2}\underline{u}aO^{2}.\end{split}

Similarly, contribution from
\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\nabla^{i_{4}}\mbox{tr}\chi
with
i_{2}=0
obeys

\begin{split}&\sum_{i\leq 4}\|\sum_{i_{3}+i_{4}=i}\Omega^{-1}u^{i+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\nabla^{i_{4}}\mbox{tr}\chi\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
\lesssim&\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}O}{|u|}\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi-(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0})\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{-2}\underline{u}aO^{2}}.\end{split}

When
i_{2}=0
, we can also control the third term
\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\frac{1}{|u|}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt
by

\begin{split}\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\underline{u}\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}L^{\infty}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim&\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}O=\Omega^{-2}\underline{u}a\cdot\frac{\Omega^{2}O}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\Omega^{-2}\underline{u}aB^{-\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

For the case
i_{2}\geq 1
, using the bootstrap assumption (
2.38
) together with Proposition
4.1
, for the first two terms we also have

\begin{split}\sum_{i\leq 4}\|\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i-1}u^{i+1}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\nabla^{i_{4}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}^{2}a^{\frac{3}{2}}O^{3}}{|u|}\leq\Omega^{-2}\underline{u}aB^{-\frac{1}{4}}.\end{split}

The third term can be bounded similarly via

\begin{split}\sum_{i\leq 4}\|\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i-1}u^{i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}^{2}aO^{2}}{|u|}\leq\Omega^{-2}\underline{u}aB^{-\frac{1}{4}}.\end{split}

Gathering above estimates, we obtain

\begin{split}&\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi-(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}_{u}L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
\lesssim&\Omega^{-2}\underline{u}aO^{2}+\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}O}{|u|}\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi-(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}_{u}L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.\end{split}

Since
\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}O/|u|\leq B^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ll 1
, we hence prove

\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi-(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}_{u}L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\Omega^{-2}\underline{u}aO^{2}.

Using
\nabla_{A}\Omega=\Omega(\eta_{A}+\underline{\eta}_{A})/2
, this further implies

\displaystyle\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi-

\displaystyle\Omega^{2}(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}_{u}L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\underline{u}aO^{2},

\displaystyle\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega

\displaystyle\mbox{tr}\chi)\|_{L^{\infty}_{u}L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}aO^{2}}{|u|}\ll a^{\frac{1}{2}}.

∎

We proceed to prove the estimates for
\hat{\omega}
and its derivatives. And
\hat{\omega}
also obeys a slightly better bound than a general component
\psi\hskip 1.0pt
.

Proposition 4.4
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
), (
2.41
), it holds

\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\hat{\omega})\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim a^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\nabla_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.

Proof.

We employ the following schematic null structure equation for
\omega
:

\Omega\nabla_{3}(\Omega\hat{\omega})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}K+\psi\hskip 1.0pt\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt+\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt+\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}+\Omega\nabla_{4}\phi\cdot\Omega\nabla_{3}\phi.

By commuting it with angular derivative for
i
times, we obtain

\begin{split}&\Omega\nabla_{3}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\hat{\omega})+\frac{i}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\hat{\omega})\\
=&\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}\leq i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}K+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+2}+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i-1}\frac{1}{|u|}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt+\frac{1}{|u|}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi,\Omega\nabla_{4}\phi).\end{split}

Note that we only encounter the fourth term when
i\geq 1
.

We then apply Proposition
3.6
with
\lambda_{0}=\frac{i}{2}
. Since
\hat{\omega}(-1,\underline{u})
=0, we control
\|u^{i-1}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\hat{\omega})\|_{L_{u}^{\infty}L_{\underline{u}}^{\infty}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
by the
\|u^{i-1}\cdot\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{\infty}L_{u}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
norm of the right hand side.

\begin{split}\mbox{Denote}\quad F_{i}:=&\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+2}+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i-1}\frac{1}{|u|}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt.\end{split}

We first have

\begin{split}\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i-1}F_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{1}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\sum_{i\leq 4}\frac{1}{|u|}\|u^{i}F_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{1}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}O^{2}}{|u|^{\frac{3}{2}}}\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|}}.\end{split}

For the terms containing
K
, if needed, we also employ
K\lesssim(K-\frac{1}{|u|^{2}})+\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}
. Applying bootstrap assumptions (
2.40
),(
2.38
) and Proposition
4.1
, their contributions obey

\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}(R+O)}{|u|^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}O^{\prime}}{|u|^{\frac{5}{2}}}\cdot\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}(R+O)\leq\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|}.

We proceed to control the contribution from
\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}
. Note that for this term, the case
i_{2}=0
happens only when
i=0
. For this scenario, the term obeys

\begin{split}\|u^{-1}\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}\|_{L_{u}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim&\frac{1}{|u|}.\end{split}

(4.2)

For
i_{2}\geq 1
, it holds

\begin{split}\sum_{i\leq 4}\|\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}=i-1}u^{i-1}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}\|_{L_{u}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}O}{|u|^{2}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|}}.\end{split}

Here in the second inequality, we employ the bootstrap assumption (
2.38
).
Collecting all the above estimates, we prove

\sum_{i\leq 4}\|\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}u^{i-1}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}(K-\frac{1}{|u|^{2}})\|_{L_{u}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{1}{|u|}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|}}.

(4.3)

For the remaining term, using the bound for
\nabla^{i}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi)
in Proposition
4.3
, we have

\begin{split}\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i-2}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi,\Omega\nabla_{4}\phi)\|_{L_{u}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim&\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|^{2}}+\frac{1}{|u|}+\frac{1}{|u|}\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\nabla_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
\lesssim&\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{3}{4}}}{|u|^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{1}{|u|}\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\nabla_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.\end{split}

(4.4)

Combining all above estimates, we obtain

\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i-1}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\hat{\omega})\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{1}{|u|}+\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|}+\frac{1}{|u|}\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\nabla_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.

Multiplying by
|u|
on both sides, we then derive

\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\hat{\omega})\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim 1+a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-\frac{1}{4}}+\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\nabla_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.

(4.5)

∎

Remark 19
.

Note that in Proposition
5.1
, we will prove

\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\nabla_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim a^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Once this is obtained, back to (
4.5
) we will get

\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\hat{\omega})\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim a^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We now estimate the
L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})
norms of the remaining Ricci coefficients.

Proposition 4.5
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
), (
2.41
), we get

\sum_{i\leq 4}\frac{1}{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim 1{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\mathcal{O}(\hat{\omega})+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(\hat{\omega})}+\mathcal{R}(\beta).

In particular, it
holds

\sum_{i\leq 4}\frac{1}{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim 1{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\mathcal{O}(\hat{\omega})+\frac{1}{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|u^{5}\nabla^{5}(\Omega\hat{\omega})\|_{L^{\infty}_{u}L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}}+\mathcal{R}(\beta).

Proof.

For
\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\in\{\eta,\nabla\log\Omega,\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})_{0},\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\Omega\underline{\omega}-(\Omega\underline{\omega})_{0}}\}
, the below schematic transport equation holds

\Omega\nabla_{4}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt=\Omega\beta{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\nabla(\Omega\hat{\omega})}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}K+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}\nabla\underline{\eta}+\psi\hskip 1.0pt\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt+\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt+\frac{1}{|u|}\psi\hskip 1.0pt.

(4.6)

By commuting the above equation with
i
angular derivatives, we have

\begin{split}\Omega\nabla_{4}\nabla^{i}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt=&\nabla^{i}(\Omega\beta)+\nabla^{i+1}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega\hat{\omega}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}\underline{\eta})+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}K\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\psi\hskip 1.0pt,\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt)+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\frac{1}{|u|}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt.\end{split}

Notice
\nabla(\Omega\underline{\omega})_{0}=0
. Due to the
naked-singularity
initial data along
\underline{u}=0
, all the quantities
\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt
are initially
0
. By Proposition
3.5
, hence to estimate the quantity
\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{\infty}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
, we only need to bound the
\|u^{i}\cdot\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
norm of the right hand side. We proceed to control each of the terms
in the equation. We bound the
\Omega\beta
term first

\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\beta)\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\beta)\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{2}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}\mathcal{R}(\beta).

(4.7)

We then estimate the term
\nabla^{i+1}(\Omega\hat{\omega})
and get

\begin{split}&\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i+1}(\Omega\hat{\omega})\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
\lesssim&\frac{\underline{u}}{|u|}\sum_{i\leq 3}\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i+1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\Omega\hat{\omega})}\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{\infty}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}\|u^{5}\nabla^{5}(\Omega\hat{\omega})\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{2}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
\lesssim&\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}(\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\hat{\omega})+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\hat{\omega})}).\end{split}

(4.8)

For the
\nabla^{i+1}\underline{\eta}
term, using the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
) and (
2.41
), it holds

\begin{split}&\sum_{i\leq 4}\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}u^{i}\nabla^{i+1}\underline{\eta}\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
\lesssim&\frac{\underline{u}}{|u|^{2}}\sum_{i\leq 3}\|u^{i+2}\nabla^{i+1}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{\infty}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|u|}}\|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}u^{5}}\nabla^{5}\underline{\eta}\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{2}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
\lesssim&\frac{\underline{u}^{2}a^{\frac{1}{2}}O}{|u|^{2}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}\cdot\Omega^{2}\cdot\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}\cdot\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}}\cdot\tilde{O}\leq\frac{\underline{u}^{2}a^{\frac{3}{4}}O}{|u|^{2}}+\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{3}{2}}a^{\frac{3}{4}}}{|u|^{\frac{3}{2}}}\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}.\end{split}

(4.9)

Employing the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.40
), we then bound the term containing the Gauss curvature
K

and we write

K=(K-\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}-\frac{1}{4}\nabla^{A}\phi\nabla_{A}\phi)+\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\nabla^{A}\phi\nabla_{A}\phi

to deduce the estimate.
It holds

\begin{split}&\sum_{i\leq 4}\|\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}u^{i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}K\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}.\end{split}

(4.10)

Applying (
2.38
) to control
\psi\hskip 1.0pt
and using Proposition
4.1
to estimate the product of
\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt
, as well as using Sobolev embedding in Proposition
3.4
, for
i\leq 4
we bound the fourth term and the final term as follows:

\begin{split}\|\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}u^{i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\psi\hskip 1.0pt,\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt)\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}^{2}aO^{2}}{|u|^{2}}\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|},\end{split}

(4.11)

\begin{split}\|\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}u^{i-1}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}(1+\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\hat{\omega}))+\frac{\underline{u}^{2}aO^{2}}{|u|^{2}}.\end{split}

(4.12)

Notice that here
\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}\psi\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq a^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\hat{\omega}))
was derived before.

Collecting all above estimates, we hence obtain

\sum_{i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\|_{L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}[1{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\hat{\omega})+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(\hat{\omega})}+\mathcal{R}(\beta)].

∎

For future use, we also prove an improved bound for
\nabla^{i}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})_{0})
for
i\geq 1
:

Proposition 4.6
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
), (
2.41
), it holds that

\sum_{1\leq i\leq 4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})_{0})\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{3}{2}}a^{\frac{3}{4}}}{|u|^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}}.

Proof.

Note that
\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})_{0}
obeys the equation

\Omega\nabla_{4}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})_{0})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}K+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}\nabla\underline{\eta}+\psi\hskip 1.0pt\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt+\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt+\frac{1}{u}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi.

By commuting the above equation with
i
angular derivatives, we have

\begin{split}&\Omega\nabla_{4}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})_{0})\\
=&\Omega^{2}\nabla^{i+1}\underline{\eta}+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\Omega^{2}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}K+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\psi\hskip 1.0pt,\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt)\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\frac{1}{|u|}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi).\end{split}

We revisit the proof of Proposition
4.5
. Except for the last term, all other terms obey the
\underline{u}^{\frac{3}{2}}a^{\frac{3}{4}}/|u|^{\frac{3}{2}}
upper bound. And for the last term, we have

\begin{split}\sum_{1\leq i\leq 4}\|\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}u^{i-1}\nabla^{i_{1}}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi)\|_{L_{\underline{u}}^{1}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{3}{2}}a^{\frac{3}{4}}}{|u|^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}}.\end{split}

Here besides using Proposition
4.1
, we also employ the improved bound for
\nabla^{i}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi)
derived in Propositions
4.3
. Collecting this with (
4.9
) and (
4.11
), we then obtain the desired conclusion.
∎

We also notice that the
\nabla_{4}
equation for
K-\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\frac{1}{4}\nabla^{A}\phi\nabla_{A}\phi}
contains exactly the same type of terms as the
\nabla_{4}
equation for
\nabla\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt
. Hence, for
\nabla^{i}(K-\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\frac{1}{4}\nabla^{A}\phi\nabla_{A}\phi})
we have the same estimates as for
\nabla^{i+1}\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt
for
i\leq 3
.

Proposition 4.7
.

With the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
) and (
2.41
), it holds

\sum_{i\leq 3}\|u^{i+2}\nabla^{i}(K-\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\frac{1}{4}\nabla^{A}\phi\nabla_{A}\phi})\|_{L^{\infty}_{u}L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}[1+\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\hat{\omega})+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(\hat{\omega})+\mathcal{R}].

In particular,

\sum_{i\leq 3}\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i}(K-\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\frac{1}{4}\nabla^{A}\phi\nabla_{A}\phi})\|_{L^{\infty}_{u}L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}[1+\sum_{i\leq 4}\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\hat{\omega})+\tilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5,2}(\hat{\omega})+\mathcal{R}]\ll 1.

5.
L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})
Estimates for Scalar Field

We now move to derive the
L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})
estimates for the scalar field. We first prove

Proposition 5.1
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
), (
2.41
), we have

\sum_{i=0}^{4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim a^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\sum_{i=0}^{4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(e_{A}\phi)\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}S}{|u|},

\sum_{i=0}^{4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega e_{3}\phi-(\Omega e_{3}\phi)_{0})\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}S}{|u|}.

Proof.

To estimate
\Omega e_{4}\phi
, we first rewrite equation (
2.8
) as

(\Omega e_{3})(\Omega e_{4}\phi)+\frac{1}{2}{\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}}\Omega e_{4}\phi=\Omega^{2}\Delta_{g}\phi-\frac{1}{2}{\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi}\Omega e_{3}\phi{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+2\Omega^{2}\eta^{A}e_{A}\phi}.

(5.1)

Commuting the above equation with
i
angular derivatives, we then obtain

\begin{split}&(\Omega\nabla_{3})\nabla^{i}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)+\frac{i+1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\nabla^{i}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\\
=&\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}\bigg(\Omega^{2}\Delta_{g}\phi-\frac{1}{2}{\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi}\Omega e_{3}\phi{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+2\Omega^{2}\eta^{A}e_{A}\phi}\bigg)\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}},\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{|u|})\nabla^{i_{4}}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i-1}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega\underline{\beta})\nabla^{i_{4}}(\Omega e_{4}\phi).\end{split}

Note that the last term only appears when
i\geq 1
.
Using Proposition
3.6
with
\lambda_{0}=\frac{i+1}{2}
, we have that
\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
is controlled by the
\|u^{i}\cdot\|{L^{1}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
norm of the right. We first estimate

\begin{split}&\|u^{i}\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega^{2}\Delta_{g}\phi)\|_{L^{1}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
\leq&\|\Omega^{2}u^{i}\nabla^{i+1}(e_{A}\phi)\|_{L^{1}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\|\Omega^{2}u^{i}\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i-1}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}+1}\nabla^{i_{3}}\Delta_{g}\phi\|_{L^{1}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}}\\
\leq&\|\Omega u^{i+1}\nabla^{i+1}(e_{A}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\|\frac{\Omega}{|u|}\|_{L^{2}_{u}L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}O}{|u|}\cdot\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}S}{|u|^{3}}|u|^{2}}\\
\leq&\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{S}}{|u|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\frac{\underline{u}^{2}aOS}{|u|^{2}}}\leq a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

Here for the last line we use bootstrap assumption

\|u^{5}\nabla^{5}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\|\Omega u^{5}\nabla^{5}(e_{A}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}S.

The terms including
-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot\Omega e_{3}\phi
are lower order and they obey

\begin{split}\|u^{i}\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot\Omega e_{3}\phi)\|_{L^{1}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq&\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|}\leq a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-1},\end{split}

where to control
\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi)\cdot\Omega e_{3}\phi\|_{L^{1}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
we employ improved estimates in Proposition (
4.3
).

Using the schematic Codazzi equation, we can rewrite
\Omega\underline{\beta}=\nabla\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt+\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}+\underline{\psi}\hskip 1.0pt)
. All the rest terms are already estimated in Proposition
4.2
and they are bounded by
a^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Hence we prove

\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq a^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.2)

To derive estimates for
e_{A}\phi
, we employ the equation

\nabla_{\Omega e_{4}}\nabla_{A}\phi=e_{A}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)-\Omega{\chi_{A}}^{B}e_{B}\phi.

Commuting this equation with
i
angular derivatives, it holds

\begin{split}\Omega D_{4}(\nabla^{i}\nabla_{A}\phi)=&\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}\nabla_{A}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega\chi)\nabla^{i_{4}}\nabla_{A}\phi\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i-1}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega\beta)\nabla^{i_{4}}\nabla_{A}\phi.\end{split}

Via Proposition
3.5
, we then bound
\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(e_{A}\phi)\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
by the
\|u^{i}\cdot\|_{L^{1}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
norm of the right hand side. With the aid of bootstrap assumption
2.40
, the term with the top-order derivative can be controlled by

\begin{split}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i+1}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{1}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq&\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i+1}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\|\frac{1}{|u|}\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
\leq&{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{S}}\cdot{\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}}=\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}\mathcal{S}.\end{split}

All the rest terms can be estimated as in Proposition
4.5
. There is neither
\nabla^{i}(\Omega\beta)
nor
\nabla^{i+1}(\Omega\hat{\omega})
. And they obey the upper bound
\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}/|u|
. Thus, we arrive at

\sum_{i=0}^{4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(e_{A}\phi)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}S}{|u|}.

To estimate
(\Omega e_{3}\phi)-(\Omega e_{3}\phi)_{0}
, using (
2.9
) and commutation formula, we get

\begin{split}&(\Omega\nabla_{4})\nabla^{i}(\Omega e_{3}\phi-(\Omega e_{3}\phi)_{0})\\
=&\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega^{2}\Delta_{g}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\Omega e_{3}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\Omega e_{4}\phi+2\Omega^{2}\eta^{A}e_{A}\phi)\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega\chi)\nabla^{i_{4}}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i-1}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega\beta)\nabla^{i_{4}}(\Omega e_{3}\phi).\end{split}

With Proposition
3.5
, we control
\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}[(\Omega e_{3}\phi)-(\Omega e_{3}\phi)_{0}]\|_{L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
by the
\|u^{i}\cdot\|_{L^{1}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
norm of the right. The term with the top-order derivative is bounded by

\begin{split}\|\Omega^{2}u^{i}\nabla^{i+1}(e_{A}\phi)\|_{L^{1}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq&\|u^{i+1}\nabla^{i+1}(e_{A}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\|\frac{\Omega^{2}}{|u|}\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\\
\leq&{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big(1+\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}\big)}\mathcal{S}\cdot{\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{S}}{|u|}.\end{split}

Here for the second inequality, we use bootstrap assumption
2.40

\|u^{5}\nabla^{5}(e_{A}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\|\Omega^{-1}u^{5}\nabla^{5}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big(1+\frac{\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|u|^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}\big)\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{4}}}\mathcal{S}.

Two borderline terms on the right are
(\Omega e_{3}\phi)\nabla^{i}(\Omega\chi)
and
(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})\nabla^{i}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)
with
i=4
. And they obey

\begin{split}\|u^{i}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)\nabla^{i}(\Omega\chi)\|_{L^{1}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq&\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\Omega\chi)\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|},\end{split}

\begin{split}\|u^{i}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})\nabla^{i}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{1}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq&\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}.\end{split}

Note that by Proposition
4.2
, Proposition
4.3
and inequality (
5.2
), we have that

\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\Omega\chi)
,
\mathcal{O}_{i,2}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)
depend only on initial data.

All the rest terms can be estimated as in Proposition
4.5
. And they are

\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}\cdot\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}\cdot(O+S)\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}B^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}.

We hence arrive at

\sum_{i=0}^{4}\|u^{i}\nabla^{i}(\Omega e_{3}\phi-(\Omega e_{3}\phi)_{0})\|_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{S}}{|u|}.

∎

6.
Energy Estimates for Scalar Field

We now move to derive the energy bounds for the top-order derivatives of the scalar field. We first recall the below covariant decompositions
12
12

12

The decompositions are the same as in
[
21
]
.

\displaystyle D_{A}e_{B}=\nabla_{A}e_{B}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{AB}e_{3}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{1}{2}}\underline{\chi}_{AB}e_{4},

(6.1)

\displaystyle D_{3}e_{A}=\nabla_{3}e_{A}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta_{A}}e_{3},\hskip 5.69054ptD_{4}e_{A}=\nabla_{4}e_{A}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ \underline{\eta}_{A}}e_{4},

(6.2)

\displaystyle D_{A}e_{3}={\underline{\chi}_{A}}^{\sharp B}e_{B}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\zeta_{A}}e_{3},\hskip 5.69054ptD_{A}e_{4}={\chi_{A}}^{\sharp B}e_{B}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\zeta_{A}}e_{4},

(6.3)

\displaystyle D_{3}e_{4}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2}\eta^{\sharp A}e_{A}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2}\hskip 1.42262pt\underline{\omega}\hskip 1.42262pte_{4},\hskip 5.69054ptD_{4}e_{3}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2}\underline{\eta}^{\sharp A}e_{A}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2}\hskip 1.42262pt\hat{\omega}\hskip 1.42262pte_{3},

(6.4)

\displaystyle D_{3}e_{3}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2}\underline{\omega}e_{3},\hskip 5.69054ptD_{4}e_{4}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2}\hat{\omega}e_{4}.

(6.5)

Next we start to use equation (
2.8
)

D_{3}D_{4}\phi=D_{4}D_{3}\phi=\Delta_{g}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi e_{3}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}e_{4}\phi.

With the fact
-2\hat{\omega}=\nabla_{4}\log\Omega=\Omega^{-1}\nabla_{4}\Omega
and the properties of covariant derivative

D_{4}D_{3}\phi=D_{4}(D_{3}\phi)-D_{D_{4}e_{3}}\phi=D_{4}(D_{3}\phi)-2\underline{\eta}^{A}e_{A}(\phi)+\Omega^{-1}D_{4}\Omega\,e_{3}\phi,

we can write (
2.8
) as

D_{4}(\Omega D_{3}\phi)-2\Omega\underline{\eta}^{A}e_{A}\phi=\Omega D_{4}D_{3}\phi=\Omega D_{3}D_{4}\phi=\Omega\Delta_{g}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi e_{3}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}e_{4}\phi.

This implies

\Omega D_{4}(\Omega D_{3}\phi)=\Omega^{2}\nabla^{A}\nabla_{A}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot\Omega e_{3}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\cdot\Omega e_{4}\phi+2\Omega^{2}\underline{\eta}^{A}e_{A}\phi.

(6.6)

Similarly, via employing the fact
-2\underline{\omega}=\nabla_{3}\log\Omega=\Omega^{-1}\nabla_{3}\Omega
and using

\begin{split}D_{3}D_{4}\phi=&D_{3}(D_{4}\phi)-D_{D_{3}e_{4}}\phi=D_{3}(D_{4}\phi)-D_{2\eta^{A}e_{A}+2\underline{\omega}e_{4}}\phi\\
=&D_{3}(D_{4}\phi)-2\eta^{A}e_{A}\phi-2\underline{\omega}e_{4}\phi=D_{3}(D_{4}\phi)-2\eta^{A}e_{A}\phi+\Omega^{-1}D_{3}\Omega e_{4}\phi,\end{split}

we also obtain

\Omega D_{3}(\Omega D_{4}\phi)=\Omega^{2}\nabla^{A}\nabla_{A}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot\Omega e_{3}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\cdot\Omega e_{4}\phi+2\Omega^{2}\eta^{A}e_{A}\phi.

(6.7)

We then proceed to derive the desired form for the transport equation of
\nabla_{A}\phi
. For
f
being a scalar function,
we define
\not{d}f
to be the
1
-form restricted to each surface
S_{u,\underline{u}}
of
df
(the differential of
f
). Denote
L,X
being vector fields, we first have

(\mathcal{L}_{L}\not{d}f)(X)=L((\not{df})(X))-(\not{d}f)[L,X]=L(Xf)-[L,X]f=X(Lf).

Letting
f=\phi,\,\,L=\frac{\partial}{\partial u}
and
X=e_{A}
, we hence obtain

(\mathcal{L}_{\frac{\partial}{\partial u}}\not{d}\phi)_{A}=\not{d}_{A}(\frac{\partial}{\partial u}\phi).

(6.8)

For
(\mathcal{L}_{\frac{\partial}{\partial u}}\not{d}\phi)_{A}
, we also have

(\mathcal{L}_{\frac{\partial}{\partial u}}\not{d}\phi)_{A}=\frac{\partial}{\partial u}(e_{A}\phi)-[\frac{\partial}{\partial u},e_{A}]\phi=\frac{\partial}{\partial u}(e_{A}\phi)-(D_{\frac{\partial}{\partial u}}e_{A})\,\phi+(D_{e_{A}}\frac{\partial}{\partial u})\,\phi=D_{\frac{\partial}{\partial u}}D_{e_{A}}\,\phi+D_{e_{A}}(\Omega e_{3})\phi.

(6.9)

For the last term, it holds

\begin{split}D_{e_{A}}(\Omega e_{3})\phi=&\Omega^{-1}e_{A}(\Omega)(\Omega e_{3}\phi)+\Omega D_{e_{A}}e_{3}\phi=e_{A}(\log\Omega)(\Omega e_{3}\phi)+\Omega({{\underline{\chi}_{A}}^{B}}e_{B}\phi+\zeta_{A}e_{3}\phi)\\
=&\eta_{A}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)+\Omega{\underline{\chi}_{A}}^{B}e_{B}\phi.\end{split}

(6.10)

Back to (
6.9
), employing (
6.8
) and (
6.10
), we get

\begin{split}D_{\frac{\partial}{\partial u}}D_{e_{A}}\,\phi=&e_{A}(\frac{\partial}{\partial u}\phi)-D_{e_{A}}(\Omega e_{3})\,\phi\\
=&e_{A}(\frac{\partial}{\partial u}\phi)-\eta_{A}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)-\Omega{\underline{\chi}_{A}}^{B}e_{B}\phi.\end{split}

(6.11)

At the same time, employing properties of the covariant derivative and the fact
D_{3}e_{A}=\nabla_{3}e_{A}+\eta_{A}e_{3}
, we also have

\begin{split}D_{\frac{\partial}{\partial u}}D_{e_{A}}\,\phi=&\frac{\partial}{\partial u}(e_{A}\phi)-D_{D_{\frac{\partial}{\partial u}e_{A}}}\phi=\frac{\partial}{\partial u}(e_{A}\phi)-D_{\nabla_{\Omega e_{3}}e_{A}+\Omega\eta_{A}e_{3}}\phi\\
=&\nabla_{\Omega e_{3}}e_{A}\phi-\Omega\eta_{A}e_{3}\phi.\end{split}

(6.12)

Comparing (
6.11
) with (
6.12
) and noting
\partial/\partial u=\Omega e_{3}
, we hence obtain

\nabla_{\Omega e_{3}}\nabla_{A}\phi=e_{A}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)-\Omega{\underline{\chi}_{A}}^{B}e_{B}\phi.

(6.13)

In the same fashion, similar to (
6.10
), we get

(D_{e_{A}}\Omega e_{4})\phi=e_{A}(\log\Omega)\Omega e_{4}\phi+\Omega({\chi_{A}}^{B}e_{B}-\zeta_{A}e_{4})\phi=\underline{\eta}_{A}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)+\Omega{\chi_{A}}^{B}e_{B}\phi.

This implies

\begin{split}D_{e_{A}}D_{\Omega e_{4}}\,\phi=e_{A}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)-(D_{e_{A}}\Omega e_{4})\,\phi=e_{A}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)-\underline{\eta}_{A}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)-\Omega{\chi_{A}}^{B}e_{B}\phi.\end{split}

(6.14)

On the other side, we also have

\begin{split}D_{\Omega e_{4}}D_{e_{A}}\,\phi=&\Omega e_{4}(e_{A}\phi)-D_{D_{\Omega e_{4}}e_{A}}\phi=\Omega e_{4}(e_{A}\phi)-D_{\nabla_{\Omega e_{4}}e_{A}+\Omega\underline{\eta}_{A}e_{4}}\phi\\
=&\nabla_{\Omega e_{4}}e_{A}\,\phi-\Omega\underline{\eta}_{A}e_{4}\phi.\end{split}

(6.15)

Employing
D_{e_{A}}D_{\Omega e_{4}}\,\phi=D_{\Omega e_{4}}D_{e_{A}}\,\phi
and collection both (
6.14
) and (
6.15
), we then arrive at

\nabla_{\Omega e_{4}}\nabla_{A}\phi=e_{A}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)-\Omega{\chi_{A}}^{B}e_{B}\phi.

(6.16)

In the rest of this section, to derive energy estimates for the scalar field, we rearrange (
6.6
), (
6.7
), (
6.13
), (
6.16
) as the below paired equations

\begin{split}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\nabla_{\Omega e_{4}}(\Omega\nabla_{A})\phi=\Omega e_{A}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)-\Omega^{2}{\hat{\chi}_{A}\,}^{B}\nabla_{B}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\nabla_{A}\phi+\Omega e_{4}(\Omega)\nabla_{A}\phi,}\end{split}

(6.17)

\begin{split}&\Omega e_{3}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)=\Omega^{2}\nabla^{A}\nabla_{A}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\cdot\Omega e_{4}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot\Omega e_{3}\phi+2\Omega^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta^{A}}\cdot e_{A}\phi,\end{split}

(6.18)

and

\begin{split}&\nabla_{\Omega e_{3}}\nabla_{A}\phi+\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\nabla_{A}\phi=e_{A}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)-\Omega{\underline{\hat{\chi}}_{A}}^{B}\nabla_{B}\phi,\end{split}

(6.19)

\begin{split}&\Omega e_{4}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)=\Omega^{2}\nabla^{A}\nabla_{A}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\cdot\Omega e_{4}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot\Omega e_{3}\phi+2\Omega^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\underline{\eta}^{A}}\cdot e_{A}\phi.\end{split}

(6.20)

For the first pair, on both sides multiplying
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}\nabla^{A}\phi
for the first equation and multiplying
\Omega e_{4}\phi
for the second equation, we get

\begin{split}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}\nabla^{A}\phi)(\Omega e_{4})({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}\nabla_{A}\phi)=&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}(\nabla^{A}\phi)e_{A}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)+\Omega\nabla^{A}\phi\nabla_{A}\phi(\Omega e_{4}\Omega)\\
&+(\Omega\nabla^{A}\phi)\cdot\big(-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}{\hat{\chi}_{A}\,}^{B}\nabla_{B}\phi-\frac{1}{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{2}}\mbox{tr}\chi\nabla_{A}\phi\big),\\
(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\Omega e_{3}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)=(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\Omega^{2}&\nabla^{A}\nabla_{A}\phi+(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\cdot\bigg(-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\cdot\Omega e_{4}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot\Omega e_{3}\phi+2\Omega^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta^{A}}\cdot e_{A}\phi\bigg).\end{split}

(6.21)

For the second pair, multiplying
\nabla^{A}\phi
for the first equation and multiplying
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{-2}}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)
for the second equation, it holds

\begin{split}\nabla^{A}\phi\nabla_{\Omega e_{3}}\nabla_{A}\phi+\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}&\nabla^{A}\phi\nabla_{A}\phi=\nabla^{A}\phi e_{A}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)-\Omega{\underline{\hat{\chi}}_{A}}^{B}\nabla_{B}\phi\nabla^{A}\phi,\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{-2}}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)\Omega e_{4}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)=&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{-2}}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)\Omega^{2}\nabla^{A}\nabla_{A}\phi\\
&+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{-2}}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)\cdot\bigg(-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\cdot\Omega e_{4}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot\Omega e_{3}\phi+2\Omega^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\underline{\eta}^{A}}\cdot e_{A}\phi\bigg).\end{split}

(6.22)

If we add the two equations in (
6.21
) and integrate on
S_{u,\underline{u}}
, we will encounter the potential top-order-derivative terms

\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\Omega^{2}(\nabla^{A}\phi)e_{A}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)+(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\Omega^{2}\nabla^{A}\nabla_{A}\phi.

(6.23)

Repeating the same procedure, for (
6.22
) we will have

\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\nabla^{A}\phi\,e_{A}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)+\Omega^{-2}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)\,\Omega^{2}\nabla^{A}\nabla_{A}\phi.

(6.24)

Conducting integration by parts with respect to
\nabla_{A}
for both (
6.23
) and (
6.24
), the potential top-order-derivative terms will be canceled.

We then recall two integration by parts formulas in
[
2
]
. Define

\begin{split}\int_{H_{u}}\phi:=&2\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}(\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}}}\Omega\phi)d\underline{u}^{\prime},\quad\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}\phi:=2\int_{-1}^{u}(\int_{S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}}}\Omega\phi)du^{\prime}\\
&\int_{D_{u,\underline{u}}}\phi:=2\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\int_{-1}^{u}(\int_{S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}}}\Omega^{2}\phi)du^{\prime}d\underline{u}^{\prime}.\end{split}

(6.25)

Applying
\phi_{1}=\Omega^{-1}\,{{}^{(1)}\phi}
and
\phi_{2}={{}^{(2)}\phi}
in Proposition 8.1 of
[
2
]
, we get

Proposition 6.1
.

Suppose
{{}^{(1)}\phi}
and
{{}^{(2)}\phi}
are
r
tensorfields, then it holds

\begin{split}&\int_{D_{u,\underline{u}}}\Omega^{-1}\,{{}^{(1)}\phi}\nabla_{4}{{}^{(2)}\phi}+\int_{D_{u,\underline{u}}}\Omega^{-1}\,{{}^{(2)}\phi}\nabla_{4}{{}^{(1)}\phi}\\
=&\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}(-1,u)}\Omega^{-1}\,{{}^{(1)}\phi}{{}^{(2)}\phi}-\int_{\underline{H}_{0}(-1,u)}\Omega^{-1}\,{{}^{(1)}\phi}{{}^{(2)}\phi}-\int_{D_{u,\underline{u}}}\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi{{}^{(1)}\phi}{{}^{(2)}\phi}.\end{split}

Set
{{}^{(1)}\phi}={{}^{(2)}\phi}=\phi
in the above proposition. Together with (
6.25
) we then obtain

Proposition 6.2
.

Suppose
\phi
is a
r
tensorfield, then it holds

\begin{split}\int_{-1}^{u}\int_{S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}}}|\phi|^{2}=\int_{-1}^{u}\int_{S_{u^{\prime},0}}|\phi|^{2}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\int_{-1}^{u}\int_{S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}}}2\phi(\Omega\nabla_{4}\phi)+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\int_{-1}^{u}\int_{S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}}}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\phi^{2}.\end{split}

By Proposition 8.2 in
[
2
]
, we also have

Proposition 6.3
.

For an
r
tensorfield
{}^{(1)}\phi
and an
r-1
tensorfield
{}^{(2)}\phi
, they satisfy

\begin{split}&\int_{D_{u,\underline{u}}}{}^{(1)}\phi^{A_{1}A_{2}...A_{r}}\nabla_{A_{r}}{}^{(2)}\phi_{A_{1}...A_{r-1}}+\int_{D_{u,\underline{u}}}\nabla^{A_{r}}{}^{(1)}\phi_{A_{1}A_{2}...A_{r}}{}^{(2)}\phi^{A_{1}...A_{r-1}}\\
=&-\int_{D_{u,\underline{u}}}(\eta+\underline{\eta}){}^{(1)}\phi{}^{(2)}\phi.\end{split}

At the same time, it also holds

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
\phi
be an
r
tensorfield and
\lambda_{1}=2(\lambda_{0}-\frac{1}{2})
. Then we get

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}^{\prime}}}|u|^{2\lambda_{1}}|\phi|^{2}=&\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\int_{S_{-1,\underline{u}^{\prime}}}|\phi|^{2}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\int_{-1}^{u}\int_{S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}}}2|u^{\prime}|^{2\lambda_{1}}<\phi,\Omega\nabla_{3}\phi+\lambda_{0}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\phi>\\
&+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\int_{-1}^{u}\int_{S_{u^{\prime},\underline{u}^{\prime}}}|u^{\prime}|^{2\lambda_{1}}(1-2\lambda_{0})(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{|u^{\prime}|})|\phi|^{2}.\end{split}

Proof.

Using the first variation of area formula, it holds

\begin{split}\frac{d}{du}(\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}|u|^{2\lambda_{1}}|\phi|^{2})=&\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}2|u|^{2\lambda_{1}}<\phi,\Omega\nabla_{3}\phi+\lambda_{0}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\phi>\\
&+\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}|u|^{2\lambda_{1}}\bigg(\frac{2\lambda_{1}\cdot(-\Omega e_{3}u)}{|u|}+(1-2\lambda_{0})\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\bigg)|\phi|^{2}.\end{split}

We then choose
\lambda_{1}=2\lambda_{0}-1
, which satisfies

\frac{2\lambda_{1}(-\Omega e_{3}u)}{|u|}+(1-2\lambda_{0})\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}=(1-2\lambda_{0})(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{|u|}).

By integrating with respect to
du\,d\underline{u}
and applying the fundamental theorem of calculus in
u
, we then prove this proposition.
∎

We now proceed to prove

Proposition 6.5
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.1
and the bootstrap assumptions (
2.38
), (
2.39
), (
2.40
), (
2.41
), it holds

\|u^{5}\nabla^{5}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+\|\Omega u^{5}\nabla^{5}(e_{A}\phi)\|_{L^{2}_{u}L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\lesssim\underline{u}^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(6.26)

Proof.

Taking equation (
6.18
)

\Omega e_{3}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)=\Omega^{2}\nabla^{A}\nabla_{A}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\cdot\Omega e_{4}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot\Omega e_{3}\phi+2\Omega^{2}\eta^{A}\cdot e_{A}\phi,

and commuting it with
i
angular derivatives, we obtain

\begin{split}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}\nabla_{3}\nabla^{i}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)+\frac{i+1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\nabla^{i}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\\
=&\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}\big(\Omega^{2}\Delta_{g}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}e_{3}\phi+2\Omega^{2}\eta^{A}e_{A}\phi\big)\\
&+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i,\\
i_{4}<i\end{subarray}}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})\nabla^{i_{4}}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}\hat{\underline{\chi}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{|u|})\nabla^{i_{4}}(\Omega e_{4}\phi)\\
&+\sum_{i_{1}+i_{2}+i_{3}+i_{4}=i-1}\nabla^{i_{1}}(\eta+\underline{\eta})^{i_{2}}\nabla^{i_{3}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega}\underline{\beta})\nabla^{i_{4}}(\Omega e_{4}\phi).\end{split}

(6.27)

Note that the term with the top-order derivative on the right is
