for brevity, proceeding the same as in the reasoning for Theorem 4.2 in
[
6
]
, we get

M\big(\frac{s}{2}\big)-m(s)\leq Ce^{4\Lambda\kappa}\Big[m\big(\frac{s}{2}\big)-m(s)\Big]+Ce^{4\Lambda\kappa+\kappa}{s}^{2},

(10.11)

M(s)-m\big(\frac{s}{2}\big)\leq Ce^{4\Lambda\kappa}\Big[M(s)-M\big(\frac{s}{2}\big)\Big]+Ce^{4\Lambda\kappa+\kappa}{s}^{2}.

(10.12)

For any
s\in(0,1)
, define

h(s):=M(s)-m(s).

(10.13)

Adding (
10.11
) and (
10.12
), we then obtain

h(s)+h\big(\frac{s}{2}\big)\leq Ce^{4\Lambda\kappa}\Big[h(s)-h\big(\frac{s}{2}\big)\Big]+Ce^{4\Lambda\kappa+\kappa}{s}^{2}.

This implies

h\big(\frac{s}{2}\big)\leq C_{\gamma}h(s)+e^{\kappa}{s}^{2}\quad\mbox{ with }\quad C_{\gamma}=\frac{Ce^{4\Lambda\kappa}-1}{Ce^{4\Lambda\kappa}+1}<1.

Using this
C_{\gamma}
, we pick up
\mu\in(0,1)
satisfying

\alpha:=(1-\mu)\log C_{\gamma}/\log(1/2)<2\mu.

Further applying Lemma 8.23 in
[
24
]
or Lemma 4.19 in
[
26
]
, for any
s\in(0,1/2]
, we hence prove

h(s)\leq C{s}^{\alpha}\big\{h(1)+e^{\kappa}s^{2\mu}\big\}\leq Ce^{\kappa}{s}^{\alpha},

(10.14)

where we employ
h(1)\leq M(1)\leq\kappa\leq e^{\kappa}
for the second inequality. This concludes (
10.9
).

∎

Next, we derive the gradient estimate for
\tilde{\phi}
. By the hyperbolic part, we have

\displaystyle|D_{\omega}a_{kl}(\omega,\tilde{\phi})|+|\partial_{\tilde{\phi}}a_{kl}(\omega,\tilde{\phi})|\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Lambda_{1}}

(10.15)

with
\Lambda_{1}
being some positive constant. The constant in the below theorem depends on
\Lambda_{1}
. And we have

Proposition 10.2
.

Set
\tilde{\phi}\in H^{1}(B_{1})
to be a positive solution to
(
10.7
) with (
10.8
) satisfied. Then, it holds

|\tilde{\phi}|_{C^{1,1/3}(B_{1/2})}\leq(Ce^{\kappa})^{Ce^{4\Lambda\kappa}}

(10.16)

with
C
being a positive constant depending only on
\Lambda

and
\Lambda_{1}
.

Proof.

The detailed steps for proving Theorem 4.3 in
[
6
]
can also be carried out here. For any function
\tilde{\psi}
belonging to
H_{0}^{1}(B_{1})\cap L^{\infty}(B_{1})
with
\tilde{\psi}\geq 0
, by (
10.7
) and (
10.6
), it holds

\displaystyle\int a_{ij}D_{i}\tilde{\phi}D_{j}\tilde{\psi}\,d\omega\leq\int\sqrt{|\gamma|}\Big[1-\frac{1}{2}fe^{\tilde{\phi}}+|D\tilde{\phi}|^{2}+1\Big]\tilde{\psi}\,d\omega\leq\int[4+2|D\tilde{\phi}|^{2}]\tilde{\psi}\,d\omega.

Taking
\underline{\xi}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\tau)}=2^{-1}\Lambda^{-1}(e^{2\Lambda\tau}-1)
, we have

\displaystyle\int a_{ij}D_{i}(\underline{\xi}(\tilde{\phi}))D_{j}\tilde{\psi}\,d\omega\leq\int 4\underline{\xi}^{\prime}(\tilde{\phi})\tilde{\psi}\,d\omega.

This is equivalent to

\displaystyle\int a_{ij}e^{2\Lambda\tilde{\phi}}D_{i}\tilde{\phi}D_{j}\tilde{\psi}\,d\omega\leq\int 4e^{2\Lambda\tilde{\phi}}\tilde{\psi}\,d\omega.

Taking
\tilde{\psi}=\tilde{\phi}\tilde{\mu}^{2}
for some
\tilde{\mu}\in C^{1}_{0}(B_{1})

with
\tilde{\mu}=1
on
B_{3/4}
, via using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we get

\int_{B_{3/4}}|D\tilde{\phi}|^{2}\,d\omega\leq C\kappa^{2}e^{2\Lambda\kappa}

(10.17)

with
C
being a positive constant depending only on
\Lambda
.

To derive a bound for the Hölder norm of
D\widetilde{\phi}
, we need to employ the regularity of
a_{ij}(\omega_{0},\tilde{\phi}(\omega_{0}))-a_{ij}(\omega,\tilde{\phi}(\omega))
, which was already obtained in Proposition
10.1
. Following the procedures of proving Theorem 4.3 in
[
6
]
, for any
0<\rho\leq s
we then obtain

\int_{B_{\rho}(\omega_{0})}|D\tilde{\phi}|^{2}\,d\omega\leq C\Big[\Big({\frac{\rho}{s}}\Big)^{2}+h(s)+[h(s)]^{2}+s^{2}\Big]\int_{B_{s}(\omega_{0})}|D\tilde{\phi}|^{2}\,d\omega+Ce^{2\kappa}{s}^{4},

where

h(s)=h(\omega_{0};s):=\sup_{B_{s}(\omega_{0})}\widetilde{\phi}-\inf_{B_{s}(\omega)}\widetilde{\phi}.

Take any
\delta\in(0,1)
. For a small
\epsilon_{\delta}>0
, we choose
s_{\delta}
small such that

B_{{s}_{\delta}}(\omega_{0})\subset B_{3/4}
and

h(s_{\delta})+[h(s_{\delta})]^{2}+s_{\delta}^{2}<\epsilon_{\delta}.

(10.18)

By employing (
10.14
) and
\kappa\gg 1
, if we set

s_{\delta}=(C_{1}e^{\kappa})^{-C_{2}e^{4\Lambda\kappa}}
,
it satisfies the above requirement.

Applying Lemma 3.4 in
[
26
]
, for any
s\leq{s}_{\delta}
we then get

\int_{B_{s}(\omega_{0})}|D\tilde{\phi}|^{2}\,d\omega\leq C{s}^{2\delta}\Big[{s}_{\delta}^{-2\delta}\int_{B_{{s}_{\delta}}(\omega_{0})}|D\tilde{\phi}|^{2}\,d\omega+e^{2\kappa}\Big].

By (
10.17
), this further implies

\int_{B_{s}(\omega_{0})}|D\tilde{\phi}|^{2}\,d\omega\leq C{s}^{2\delta}\big[\kappa^{2}e^{2\Lambda\kappa}{s}_{\delta}^{-2\delta}+e^{2\kappa}\big].

(10.19)

Proceeding the same as in
[
6
]
,
defining

(D\tilde{\phi})_{\omega_{0},s}:=\frac{1}{|B_{s}(\omega_{0})|}\int_{B_{s}(\omega_{0})}D\tilde{\phi},

it also holds

\displaystyle\int_{B_{s}(\omega_{0})}|D\tilde{\phi}-(D\tilde{\phi})_{\omega_{0},s}|^{2}\,d\omega\leq C\kappa^{3}e^{3\Lambda\kappa}{s}_{\delta}^{-3\delta}{s}^{2+2\alpha^{\prime}}

if we further impose

\delta\in(2/3,1)
and
\alpha^{\prime}=3\delta/2-1
.

Applying Theorem 3.1 in
[
26
]
, together with (
10.17
), we deduce that, for any
\omega\in B_{1/2}

|D\tilde{\phi}(\omega)|\leq C\big[\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{\frac{3}{2}\Lambda\kappa}{s}_{\delta}^{-\frac{3\delta}{2}}+\kappa e^{\Lambda\kappa}\big]\leq C\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{\frac{3}{2}\Lambda\kappa}{s}_{\delta}^{-\frac{3\delta}{2}},

(10.20)

and for any
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in B_{1/2}
satisfying
|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|<{s}_{\delta}
,

|D\tilde{\phi}(\omega_{1})-D\tilde{\phi}(\omega_{2})|\leq C\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{\frac{3}{2}\Lambda\kappa}{s}_{\delta}^{-\frac{3\delta}{2}}|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|^{\alpha^{\prime}}.

In addition, for any
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in B_{1/2}
we also have

|D\tilde{\phi}(\omega_{1})-D\tilde{\phi}(\omega_{2})|\leq C\kappa^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{\frac{3}{2}\Lambda\kappa}{s}_{\delta}^{-\frac{3\delta}{2}}{s}_{\delta}^{-\alpha^{\prime}}|\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}|^{\alpha^{\prime}}.

(10.21)

Recall that
{s}_{\delta}=(C_{1}e^{\kappa})^{-C_{2}e^{4\Lambda\kappa}}

and we have
0<\alpha^{\prime}<1/2
. We now choose
\alpha^{\prime}=1/3
. Utilizing (
10.20
) and (
10.21
), we hence conclude

|D\tilde{\phi}|_{C^{1/3}(B_{1/2})}\leq(Ce^{\kappa})^{Ce^{2\Lambda\kappa}}.\\

(10.22)

∎

We further prove the following Schauder estimates.
We work under the assumption

\displaystyle|D^{2}_{\omega}a_{kl}(\omega,\tilde{\phi})|+|D_{\omega}\partial_{\tilde{\phi}}a_{kl}(\omega,\tilde{\phi})|+|\partial^{2}_{\tilde{\phi}}a_{kl}(\omega,\tilde{\phi})|\leq\Lambda_{2}

(10.23)

with
\Lambda_{2}
being a positive constant.

Proposition 10.3
.

Suppose
\tilde{\phi}\in H^{1}(B_{1})
is a positive solution to
(
10.7
) with (
10.8
) satisfied, and
f\in C^{1/3}(B_{1})
.
Then, it holds

|\tilde{\phi}|_{C^{2,1/3}(B_{1/2})}\leq(Ce^{\kappa})^{Ce^{4\Lambda\kappa}}.

(10.24)

Here
C
is a positive constant depending only on
\Lambda
,

\Lambda_{1}
,
\Lambda_{2}
,
the
C^{1}
-norms of
\sqrt{|\gamma|}
,
c_{ij}
,
c_{i}
, and
c_{0}
on

B_{1}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

and the
C^{1/3}
-norm of
f
on
B_{1}
.

Proof.

We have already proved
\widetilde{\phi}\in C^{1,1/3}(B_{1/2})
and we can write (
10.7
) in nondivergence form

a_{ij}(\omega,\tilde{\phi})\partial_{ij}\tilde{\phi}={h}(\omega)

(10.25)

with

{h}(\omega)=-\partial_{j}a_{ij}(\omega,\tilde{\phi})\partial_{i}\tilde{\phi}-\partial_{\tilde{\phi}}a_{ij}(\omega,\tilde{\phi})\partial_{i}\tilde{\phi}\partial_{j}\tilde{\phi}-\sqrt{|\gamma|}\Big[1-\frac{1}{2}fe^{\tilde{\phi}}+F(\omega,\tilde{\phi},D\widetilde{\phi})\Big].

We now treat equation (
10.25
) as a linear equation of
\tilde{\phi}
, and consider
{h}
as a given term defined in
B_{1}
.
Using the expression of
F
and Proposition
10.2
, we get

|{h}|_{C^{1/3}(B_{3/4})}\leq(Ce^{\kappa})^{Ce^{4\Lambda\kappa}}.

Employing the standard interior Schauder estimate, we then obtain

|\tilde{\phi}|_{C^{2,1/3}(B_{1/2})}\leq C_{*}\big[|\tilde{\phi}|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3/4})}+|h|_{C^{1/3}(B_{3/4})}\big]\leq C_{*}(Ce^{\kappa})^{Ce^{4\Lambda\kappa}}

with
C_{*}>0
a constant depending only on
\Lambda
, and the
C^{1/3}
-norm of
a_{ij}
on
B_{3/4}
.
∎

Gathering the above three propositions just obtained, we are ready to prove

Theorem 10.4
.

With
\mathcal{S}(\tilde{\phi})
being defined as in (
9.4
) and
\kappa
being a positive constant, if we assume
\tilde{\phi}
is a solution to
\mathcal{S}(\tilde{\phi})=0
satisfying

0\leq\tilde{\phi}\leq\kappa
on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
,
then it holds

|\tilde{\phi}|_{C^{2,1/3}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\leq(Ce^{\kappa})^{Ce^{4\Lambda\kappa}},

with
\Lambda
being the ellipticity constant for the metric component
\gamma_{ij}
and
C
being a
positive constant depending only on

\gamma,\hat{\underline{\chi}},\eta,\,\alpha^{\prime}_{1}=-(\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}_{g}\underline{\chi}+\frac{1}{R}),\,\alpha_{2}=\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}_{g}\chi-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{R}+\frac{\underline{u}af(\omega,\underline{u})}{2\Omega^{2}_{0}R^{2}}(1-\mu^{*})

and their angular derivatives.

Proof.

We first fix an arbitrary ball
B
on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
. In local coordinates within
B
,
we have (
10.5
) and

a_{ij}(\omega,\tilde{\phi})=\sqrt{|\gamma(u,\omega)|}\gamma^{ij}(u,\omega).

Here
u=-R=-(\underline{u}a)^{1-\mu^{*}}e^{-(1-\mu^{*})\tilde{\phi}}.
Recall that

F=F_{1}+F_{2}
with
F_{1}=-4(1-\mu^{*})R\Omega\underline{\omega}|\nabla_{\gamma}\tilde{\phi}|^{2}
and
F_{2}
being a linear combination of
\nabla_{i}\tilde{\phi}\nabla_{j}\tilde{\phi},\nabla_{i}\tilde{\phi},1
with the below coefficients

\gamma^{ij},\quad R^{-1}\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}_{kl},\quad\eta_{i},\quad R\alpha^{\prime}_{1},\quad R\alpha_{2}.

The coefficients of
F_{2}
are functions of
\omega
and
u=-R=-(\underline{u}a)^{1-\mu^{*}}e^{-(1-\mu^{*})\tilde{\phi}}
. To control the
C^{1/3}
-norm of
F_{2}
, we estimate these coefficients and their derivatives with respect to
\omega
and
\tilde{\phi}
.
With
\partial_{\tilde{\phi}}=R\partial_{u}
, we hence need to bound

\displaystyle R^{-1}\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}_{kl},\,\eta_{k},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R\alpha^{\prime}_{1}},\,R\alpha_{2},

\displaystyle R^{-1}\nabla_{i}(\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}_{kl}),\,\nabla_{i}\eta_{k},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R\nabla_{i}\alpha^{\prime}_{1}},\,R\nabla_{i}\alpha_{2},

\displaystyle\partial_{u}(\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}_{kl}),\,R\partial_{u}\eta_{k},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R^{2}\partial_{u}\alpha^{\prime}_{1}},\,R^{2}\partial_{u}\alpha_{2}

with

\alpha^{\prime}_{1}:=-(\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}_{g}\underline{\chi}+\frac{1}{R}),\quad\alpha_{2}:=\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}_{g}\chi-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{R}+\frac{\underline{u}af(\underline{u},\omega)}{2\Omega^{2}_{0}R^{2}}(1-\mu^{*}).

(10.26)

By the hyperbolic estimates, all these quantities are bounded by
e^{\tilde{\phi}}a^{-\frac{1}{3}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{\tilde{\delta}}}
.
The conditions in Proposition
10.1
,
Proposition
10.2
, and Proposition
10.3
all hold. Therefore, we derive the desired
C^{2,1/3}
estimates for solution
\widetilde{\phi}
to the equation of MOTS.
∎

11.
The Linearized Equation

To derive and to analyze the linearized equation, in contrast to
[
6
]
and previous sections, here we recast the equation of MOTS with a different form and carry out the associated linear analysis.

For
u=-R(\omega,\underline{u})
being the MOTS along
\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}
, we set
R=(\underline{u}a)^{1-\mu^{*}}\check{\phi}^{\lambda}
with
\lambda
to be determined later. Then, it holds

\nabla_{g}R=\lambda(\underline{u}a)^{1-\mu^{*}}\check{\phi}^{\lambda-1}\nabla_{g}\check{\phi}=\lambda R\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla_{g}\check{\phi},

which implies

\displaystyle\mbox{div }(\nabla_{g}R)=

\displaystyle\mbox{div }(\lambda R\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla_{g}\check{\phi})=(\lambda^{2}-\lambda)R\check{\phi}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-2}}|\nabla_{g}\check{\phi}|^{2}+\lambda R\check{\phi}^{-1}\Delta_{g}\check{\phi}.

Thus, with the aid of (
9.1
), the equation of MOTS
0=\mathcal{G}(R,\underline{u})
is changed into

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\Delta_{g}R+\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}_{g}\underline{\chi}|\nabla_{g}R|^{2}+4\Omega\underline{\omega}|\nabla_{g}R|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}_{g}\chi+2g(\eta,\nabla_{g}R){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+2\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}(\nabla_{g}R,\nabla_{g}R)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[-\lambda+\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\lambda^{2}]R\check{\phi}^{-2}|\nabla_{g}\check{\phi}|^{2}+\lambda R\check{\phi}^{-1}\Delta_{g}\check{\phi}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}_{g}\chi+2\lambda R\check{\phi}^{-1}g(\eta,\nabla_{g}\check{\phi})

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+(\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}_{g}\underline{\chi}+\frac{1}{R})\lambda^{2}R^{2}\check{\phi}^{-2}|\nabla_{g}\check{\phi}|^{2}+[4\Omega\underline{\omega}-\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\cdot\frac{1}{R}]\lambda^{2}R^{2}\check{\phi}^{-2}|\nabla_{g}\check{\phi}|^{2}+2\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}(\nabla_{g}R,\nabla_{g}R).}

We now pick

\lambda=\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}},\mbox{ which satisfies }-\lambda+\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\lambda^{2}=0.

This renders the above equation to be

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\lambda R\check{\phi}^{-1}\Delta_{g}\check{\phi}-\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}_{g}\chi+2\lambda R\check{\phi}^{-1}g(\eta,\nabla_{g}\check{\phi})+(\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}_{g}\underline{\chi}+\frac{1}{R})\lambda^{2}R^{2}\check{\phi}^{-2}|\nabla_{g}\check{\phi}|^{2}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+[4\Omega\underline{\omega}-\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\cdot\frac{1}{R}]\lambda^{2}R^{2}\check{\phi}^{-2}|\nabla_{g}\check{\phi}|^{2}+2\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}(\nabla_{g}R,\nabla_{g}R).}

With Christodoulou’s naked-singularity initial data, we note that
4\Omega\underline{\omega}(-R,0)=\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\cdot\frac{1}{R}
. Hence we further define
\alpha_{1}
as below and recall
\alpha_{2}
given in (
10.26
)

\alpha_{1}:=-(\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}_{g}\underline{\chi}+\frac{1}{R}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-[4\Omega\underline{\omega}-(4\Omega\underline{\omega})_{0}],}

\alpha_{2}:=\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}_{g}\chi-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{R}+\frac{\underline{u}af(\omega,\underline{u})}{2\Omega^{2}_{0}R^{2}}(1-\mu^{*}).

Together with
R=(\underline{u}a)^{1-\mu^{*}}\check{\phi}^{\lambda}
,
\gamma=R^{-2}g
,
\Delta_{g}\check{\phi}=R^{-2}\Delta_{\gamma}\check{\phi}
and
\Omega^{2}_{0}(R)=\Omega^{2}(u,0)=R^{\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}}
, the above equation infers

\displaystyle\lambda\check{\phi}^{-1}\Delta_{\gamma}\check{\phi}-(1-\mu^{*})+(1-\mu^{*})\frac{f(\omega,\underline{u})}{2}\check{\phi}^{-\frac{\lambda}{1-\mu^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+}2\lambda R^{2}\check{\phi}^{-1}g(\eta,\nabla_{g}\check{\phi})

\displaystyle-\lambda^{2}R^{3}\check{\phi}^{-2}|\nabla_{g}\check{\phi}|^{2}\alpha_{1}-R\alpha_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+2R\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}(\nabla_{g}R,\nabla_{g}R)}=0.

With
\lambda=(1-\mu^{*})/\mu^{*}
, we now define

\begin{split}S(\check{\phi},\omega):=&\Delta_{\gamma}\check{\phi}-\mu^{*}\check{\phi}+\frac{\mu^{*}}{2}f(\omega,\underline{u})\check{\phi}^{1-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+}\frac{2\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\lambda R^{2}g(\eta,\nabla_{g}\check{\phi})\\
&-\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\lambda^{2}R^{3}\check{\phi}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-1}}|\nabla_{g}\check{\phi}|^{2}\alpha_{1}-\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\check{\phi}R\alpha_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\frac{2\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\check{\phi}R\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}(\nabla_{g}R,\nabla_{g}R)}.\end{split}

(11.1)

And for
2
-sphere
S_{-(\underline{u}a)^{1-\mu^{*}}\check{\phi}^{\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}},\,\underline{u}}
, we have that, if

S(\check{\phi},\omega)\begin{cases}<0,&\text{it corresponds to an untrapped surface},\\
=0,&\text{it corresponds to a MOTS},\\
>0,&\text{it corresponds to a trapped surface}.\end{cases}

For the operator
\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi},\omega)
defined in (
11.1
), it further holds

Proposition 11.1
.

Suppose
\check{\phi}
is a given function on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.
The linearized operator
\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})
can be expressed as

\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})[w]=\Delta_{\gamma}w-\mu^{*}w-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{2}f(\omega)\check{\phi}^{-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}w+c^{i}\nabla_{i}w+cw.

(11.2)

Here the connection is with respect to the metric
\gamma
, and functions

c=c(\omega,\check{\phi},\underline{u})
and
c^{i}=c^{i}(\omega,\check{\phi},\underline{u})
satisfy

|c|+|c^{i}|\leq Ca^{-\frac{1}{3}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{\tilde{\delta}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(|\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla^{2}_{\gamma}\check{\phi}|+|\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla_{\gamma}\check{\phi}|^{2}+1)}.

Proof.

We first write

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}+I_{5},

(11.3)

with

\displaystyle I_{1}=\Delta_{\gamma}\check{\phi}-\mu^{*}\check{\phi}+\frac{\mu^{*}}{2}f\check{\phi}^{1-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}},

(11.4)

\displaystyle I_{2}=\frac{2\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda^{2}\check{\phi}^{-1}}R^{-1}\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}\nabla_{j}\check{\phi},\quad

\displaystyle I_{3}=\frac{2\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\lambda\gamma^{ij}\eta_{i}\nabla_{j}\check{\phi},

\displaystyle I_{4}=-\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda^{2}\check{\phi}^{-1}}R\alpha_{1}|\nabla_{\gamma}\check{\phi}|^{2}_{\gamma},\quad

\displaystyle I_{5}=-\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\check{\phi}R\alpha_{2}.

(11.5)

Then we take
\partial_{\check{\phi}}
with respect to each
I_{i}
with
i=1,2,3,4,5
. Recall
R=(\underline{u}a)^{1-\mu^{*}}\check{\phi}^{\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}}
and we set

R(\epsilon)=(\underline{u}a)^{1-\mu^{*}}(\check{\phi}+\epsilon w)^{\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}}.

Denoting
\dot{\,}=\frac{d\,}{d\epsilon}\Big|_{\epsilon=0}
, it then holds

\dot{R}=R\check{\phi}^{-1}\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}w,\quad\dot{u}=-R\check{\phi}^{-1}\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}w,\quad\dot{\check{\phi}}=w.

We also have

\dot{\gamma}^{ij}=-\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\dot{\gamma}_{kl}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\dot{\gamma}_{kl}=\dot{u}\partial_{u}\gamma_{kl}=-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}\check{\phi}^{-1}w[(R\Omega\mbox{tr}_{g}\underline{\chi}+2)\gamma_{kl}+2R^{-1}\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}_{kl}].

For
I_{1}=\Delta_{\gamma}\check{\phi}-\mu^{*}\check{\phi}+\frac{\mu^{*}}{2}f\check{\phi}^{1-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}
, we further apply Lemma
9.1
to
\gamma
. Together with
\dot{\phi}=w
, we obtain

\begin{split}(\Delta_{\gamma}\check{\phi})^{\bm{\cdot}}=&\Delta_{\gamma}w-\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\dot{\gamma}_{kl}\nabla^{2}_{ij}\check{\phi}-\gamma^{ij}\gamma^{kl}\nabla_{i}\dot{\gamma}_{jl}\nabla_{k}\check{\phi}+\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{ij}\gamma^{kl}\nabla_{l}\dot{\gamma}_{ij}\nabla_{k}\check{\phi}\end{split}

(11.6)

and

\dot{I}_{1}=\Delta_{\gamma}w-\mu^{*}w-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{2}f(\omega)\check{\phi}^{-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}w.

For
I_{2}=\frac{2\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda^{2}\check{\phi}^{-1}}R^{-1}\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}\nabla_{j}\check{\phi}
, with
\lambda=\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}
it holds

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{2}=

\displaystyle-2\lambda\check{\phi}^{-2}wR^{-1}\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}\nabla_{j}\check{\phi}-2\lambda\check{\phi}^{-1}R^{-2}R\check{\phi}^{-1}\lambda w\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}\nabla_{j}\check{\phi}

\displaystyle+2\lambda\check{\phi}^{-1}R^{-1}\partial_{u}(\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}_{kl})\cdot(-R\check{\phi}^{-1}\lambda w)\cdot\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}\nabla_{j}\check{\phi}+2\lambda\check{\phi}^{-1}R^{-1}\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}_{kl}(\dot{\gamma}^{ik}\gamma^{jl}+\gamma^{ik}\dot{\gamma}^{jl})\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}\nabla_{j}\check{\phi}

\displaystyle+2\lambda\check{\phi}^{-1}R^{-1}\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}(\nabla_{i}w\nabla_{j}\check{\phi}+\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}\nabla_{j}w),

where the coefficient

for

and the coefficient

for

satisfying

For
I_{3}=-\frac{2\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\gamma^{ij}\eta_{i}\nabla_{j}\check{\phi}
, we get

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{3}=

\displaystyle\frac{2\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\bigg(-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}\check{\phi}^{-1}w[(R\Omega\mbox{tr}_{g}\underline{\chi}+2)\gamma_{kl}+2R^{-1}\Omega\hat{\underline{\chi}}_{kl}]\bigg)\eta_{i}\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}

\displaystyle-\frac{2\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\gamma^{ij}\partial_{u}\eta_{i}\cdot(-R\check{\phi}^{-1}\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}w)\nabla_{j}\check{\phi}-\frac{2\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\gamma^{ij}\eta_{i}\nabla_{j}w.

And on the right the coefficient

For
I_{4}=-\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda^{2}\check{\phi}^{-1}}R\alpha_{1}|\nabla_{\gamma}\check{\phi}|^{2}
, we have the expression

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{4}=

\displaystyle\lambda\check{\phi}^{-1}R\partial_{u}\alpha_{1}R\check{\phi}^{-1}\lambda w\gamma^{ij}\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}\nabla_{j}\check{\phi}+\lambda\check{\phi}^{-2}wR\alpha_{1}\gamma^{ij}\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}\nabla_{j}\check{\phi}

\displaystyle-\lambda\check{\phi}^{-1}R\check{\phi}^{-1}\lambda w\alpha_{1}\gamma^{ij}\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}\nabla_{j}\check{\phi}-\lambda\check{\phi}^{-1}R\alpha_{1}\dot{\gamma}^{ij}\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}\nabla_{j}\check{\phi}

\displaystyle-\lambda\check{\phi}^{-1}R\alpha_{1}\gamma^{ij}(\nabla_{i}w\nabla_{j}\check{\phi}+\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}\nabla_{j}w).

With precise calculations, we show that the size of the coefficients for
\omega
and
\nabla_{i}\omega
are small. First recall

\partial_{u}\alpha_{1}=-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{u}[\Omega\mbox{tr}_{g}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{R}]-4\partial_{u}[\Omega\underline{\omega}-(\Omega\underline{\omega})_{0}].

For the term
-\partial_{u}[\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}_{g}\underline{\chi}+\frac{1}{R}]
, there are cancellations. By the null structure equation and
\partial_{u}(2R^{-1})-2R^{-2}=0
, it holds

\begin{split}&\partial_{u}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{R})+\frac{1}{2}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-\frac{2}{R})(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{R})\\
=&-4\Omega\underline{\omega}\cdot\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-\Omega^{2}|\hat{\underline{\chi}}|^{2}_{g}-(\partial_{u}\phi)^{2}\\
=&-4[\Omega\underline{\omega}-(\Omega\underline{\omega})_{0}]\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-4(\Omega\underline{\omega})_{0}[\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})_{0}]-\Omega^{2}|\hat{\underline{\chi}}|^{2}_{g}-[\partial_{u}\phi-(\partial\phi)_{0}]\partial_{u}\phi\\
&-(\partial_{u}\phi)_{0}[\partial_{u}\phi-(\partial_{u}\phi)_{0}]-4(\Omega\underline{\omega})_{0}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})_{0}-(\partial_{u}\phi)_{0}(\partial_{u}\phi)_{0}.\end{split}

Using the hyperbolic part, we have improved estimates

|\Omega\underline{\omega}-(\Omega\underline{\omega})_{0}|\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|^{2}},\quad|\partial_{u}\phi-(\partial_{u}\phi)_{0}|\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|^{2}}.

The remaining worrisome terms are

-4(\Omega\underline{\omega})_{0}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})_{0}-(\partial_{u}\phi)_{0}(\partial_{u}\phi)_{0}.

They could be of size
R^{-2}
and not small, which is dangerous. However, cancellations of Christodoulou’s naked-singularity initial data help us. Along
\underline{u}=0
, we have

\displaystyle-4(\Omega\underline{\omega})_{0}\cdot(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})_{0}-(\partial_{u}\phi)_{0}^{2}=

\displaystyle-4\frac{\mu^{*}}{4(1-\mu^{*})}\cdot\frac{1}{R}\cdot(\frac{-2}{R})-2\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\cdot\frac{1}{R^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\cdot\frac{1}{R^{2}}-\frac{2\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}\cdot\frac{1}{R^{2}}=0.

Hence, the worrisome terms disappear.
15
15

15

For perturbed initial data, after the cancellation of the leading terms, these worrisome items are of size
\Omega(-R,0)^{\tilde{\delta}}R^{-2}a^{-\frac{1}{3}}
, which are sufficiently small.

The term
-4\partial_{u}[\Omega\underline{\omega}-(\Omega\underline{\omega})_{0}]
in
\partial_{u}\alpha_{1}
also encodes this smallness. We have

\displaystyle\partial_{u}[\Omega\underline{\omega}-(\Omega\underline{\omega})_{0}]=

\displaystyle\partial_{u}[\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}(\Omega\nabla_{4})(\Omega\underline{\omega})(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})d\underline{u}^{\prime}]=\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\partial_{u}(\Omega\nabla_{4})(\Omega\underline{\omega})(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})d\underline{u}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}[\Omega\nabla_{3}(-\Omega^{2}\eta\cdot\underline{\eta}+\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{2}|\eta|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{2}\rho{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\frac{1}{6}\Omega e_{3}\phi\cdot\Omega e_{4}\phi+\frac{1}{12}\Omega^{2}e_{A}\phi e^{A}\phi})](u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})d\underline{u}^{\prime}.

In the last line of above equation, except
\Omega e_{3}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)
and
\nabla_{3}\eta
, all the other terms can be explicitly expressed and obey desired estimates. To control
\Omega\nabla_{3}(\Omega\nabla_{3}\phi)
term, for
h
being a scalar function, we recall a fact

\Omega\nabla_{4}(\Omega\nabla_{3}h)=\Omega\nabla_{3}(\Omega\nabla_{4}h)-4\Omega^{2}\zeta\cdot\nabla h.

Setting
h=\Omega e_{3}\phi
, we then have

\displaystyle\Omega e_{4}(\Omega e_{3})(\Omega e_{3}\phi)=

\displaystyle\Omega e_{3}(\Omega e_{4})(\Omega e_{3}\phi)-4\Omega^{2}\zeta\nabla(\Omega e_{3}\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Omega e_{3}[-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\cdot\Omega e_{4}\phi-\Omega^{2}\Delta_{g}\phi-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot\Omega e_{3}\phi+2\Omega^{2}\underline{\eta}^{A}e_{A}\phi]-4\Omega^{2}\zeta\nabla(\Omega e_{3}\phi).

Using naked-singularity initial data and integrating along
e_{4}
direction, we get

|\Omega e_{3}(\Omega e_{3}\phi)|\lesssim\frac{1}{|u|^{2}},

which is a desired estimate for here. To bound
\nabla_{3}\eta
term, we utilize

\nabla_{3}\eta=-\nabla_{3}\underline{\eta}+2\nabla_{3}\nabla\log\Omega.

There is the null structure equation for
\nabla_{3}\underline{\eta}
and we also have

\nabla_{3}\nabla\log\Omega=\nabla\nabla_{3}\log\Omega+\frac{1}{2}(\eta+\underline{\eta})\nabla_{3}\log\Omega-\underline{\chi}\cdot\nabla\log\Omega=-2\nabla\underline{\omega}-(\eta+\underline{\eta})\underline{\omega}-\underline{\chi}\cdot\nabla\log\Omega.

These imply

|\nabla_{3}\eta|\leq\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|^{3}}\leq\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}\cdot\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{\tilde{\delta}}}}{a^{\frac{1}{3}}},

which is a desired estimate for here as well.

With the derived estimate above, a direct check shows that, for
\dot{I}_{4}
the coefficient
c
for
w
and the coefficient
c_{i}
for
\nabla_{i}w
obey

|c|\leq\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{\tilde{\delta}}}}{a^{\frac{1}{3}}}(\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla_{j}\check{\phi})^{2},\quad|c_{i}|\leq\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{\tilde{\delta}}}}{a^{\frac{1}{3}}}|\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla_{\gamma}\check{\phi}|.

We proceed to study
\dot{I}_{5}
. For
I_{5}=-\lambda^{-1}\check{\phi}R\alpha_{2}
with

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}=

\displaystyle[\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}_{g}\chi-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{R}+\frac{\underline{u}af(\omega,\underline{u})}{2\Omega^{2}_{0}R^{2}}(1-\mu^{*})](u,\underline{u},\omega)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}(\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\mbox{tr}\chi+|\hat{\chi}|_{g}^{2}+\partial_{4}\phi\partial_{4}\phi)(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\frac{\Omega^{-2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)}{|u|^{2}}\bigg(|\Omega\hat{\chi}|_{g}^{2}(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)+[\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)-\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)]^{2}\bigg)d\underline{u}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\frac{\Omega^{-2}}{2}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi)^{2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\frac{\Omega^{-2}}{|u|^{2}}[|u\Omega\hat{\chi}|^{2}_{g}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)-|\Omega\hat{\chi}|^{2}_{g}(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)]d\underline{u}^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\frac{\Omega^{-2}}{|u|^{2}}\bigg((u\Omega e_{4}\phi)^{2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)-[\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)-\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)]^{2}\bigg)d\underline{u}^{\prime},

it holds

\displaystyle\dot{I}_{5}=\lambda^{-1}\check{\phi}R\partial_{u}\alpha_{2}\cdot R\check{\phi}^{-1}\lambda w-\lambda^{-1}wR\alpha_{2}-\lambda^{-1}\check{\phi}R\check{\phi}^{-1}\lambda w\alpha_{2}.

To bound
\partial_{u}\alpha_{2}
, we first control

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\partial_{u}\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot\mbox{tr}\chi(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}\\
=&\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\bigg(-\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\mbox{tr}\chi+2\Omega\underline{\omega}\mbox{tr}\chi+2\Omega\rho-\Omega\hat{\chi}\cdot\hat{\underline{\chi}}\bigg)\mbox{tr}\chi(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}\\
&+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\bigg(2\Omega\mbox{div }\eta+2\Omega|\eta|^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\frac{1}{3}\Omega e_{3}\phi e_{4}\phi+\frac{1}{3}\Omega e_{A}\phi e^{A}\phi}\bigg)\mbox{tr}\chi(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}\end{split}

(11.7)

Applying the improved estimate for
\mbox{tr}\chi
, we bound the potentially dangerous terms

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\Omega\underline{\omega},\Omega e_{3}\phi)\cdot(\mbox{tr}\chi,e_{4}\phi)\cdot\mbox{tr}\chi(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\underline{u}\Omega^{-2}}{|u|}\cdot\frac{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}\cdot(\frac{\Omega}{|u|}+\Omega^{-1}\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|^{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\cdot\frac{a^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Omega}{|u|}+\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{|u|}\cdot\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|\Omega}\leq\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}\cdot\frac{\Omega^{\tilde{\delta}}}{a^{\frac{1}{3}}}.

All the other terms in (
11.7
) obey even smaller upper bound.

For the remaining terms in
\partial_{u}\alpha_{2}
, we need to show that
16
16

16

With perturbed initial data, we would get a similar bound.

||u|\Omega e_{4}\phi(u,\underline{u},\omega)-[\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)-\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)]|\leq a^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot a^{-\frac{1}{3}}\Omega^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tilde{\delta}}}.

(11.8)

A detailed discussion in Section
13.1
, in particular (
13.2
), will give

|u\Omega e_{4}\phi(u,\underline{u},\omega)-[\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)-\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)]|\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|}+|u|^{\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}}\leq a^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot a^{-\frac{1}{3}}\Omega^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tilde{\delta}}}.

With these estimates, we can check that
\partial_{u}\alpha_{2}
obeys

|\partial_{u}\alpha_{2}|\leq\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{\tilde{\delta}}}}{a^{\frac{1}{3}}}.

Back to
\dot{I}_{5}
, we conclude the coefficient
c
for
w
and the coefficient
c_{i}
for
\nabla_{i}w
obey

|c|+|c_{i}|\leq\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{\tilde{\delta}}}}{a^{\frac{1}{3}}}.

∎

For the next step, we set

L_{0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}w}:=\Delta_{\gamma}w-\mu^{*}w+\frac{\mu^{*}-1}{2}f(\omega,\underline{u})\check{\phi}^{-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}w.

Integrating
-wL_{0}w
on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
and applying integration by parts, we obtain

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mu^{*}w^{2}d\omega\leq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\bigg(|\nabla_{\gamma}w|^{2}+\mu^{*}w^{2}+\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{2}f(\omega,\underline{u})\check{\phi}^{-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}w^{2}\bigg)d\omega=-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}wL_{0}wd\omega.

This implies

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}w^{2}d\omega\leq C\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|L_{0}w|^{2}d\omega.

We further let

<w_{1},w_{2}>:=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\bigg(\nabla_{\gamma}w_{1}\cdot\nabla_{\gamma}w_{2}+\mu^{*}w_{1}w_{2}+\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{2}f(\omega,\underline{u})\check{\phi}^{-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}w_{1}w_{2}\bigg)d\omega.

Since
0<\mu^{*}<1
and
f\geq 0
, we can use
<w_{1},w_{2}>
as an inner-product on
H^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
, equivalent to the standard
H^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
inner product. The first eigenvalue of
-L_{0}
can be expressed as

\mu_{1}=\inf\{\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}[|\nabla_{\gamma}w|^{2}+\mu^{*}w^{2}+\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{2}f(\omega,\underline{u})\check{\phi}^{-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}w^{2}]d\omega;\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}w^{2}d\omega=1\}.

Immediately, we see that
\mu_{1}\geq\mu^{*}
. And via using Theorem 8.38 in
[
24
]
, we also have

Lemma 11.2
.

Assume
f
and
\check{\phi}
to be given nonnegative functions on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
with (
1.4
) satisfied. Then the equation
L_{0}w=0
only admits the trivial solution. Moreover, the first eigenvalue
\mu_{1}
of
-L_{0}
is positive and simple and it has a positive eigenfunction.

With this property, we obtain the crucial invertibility of the operator
\partial_{\check{\phi}}S(\check{\phi})
:

Lemma 11.3
.

Let
\beta\in(0,1)
be a fixed constant. Set

f
and
\check{\phi}
to be given nonnegative functions on
\mathbb{S}^{2}

with (
1.4
) satisfied and

|\check{\phi}|_{C^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\leq K
with
K
obeying (
9.14
). Then within the hyperbolic region, it holds that

\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi}):C^{2,\beta}(\mathbb{S}^{2})\rightarrow C^{{0},\beta}(\mathbb{S}^{2})

is invertible.

Proof.

Applying Lemma
11.2
, we first take a positive
eigenfunction
\psi_{1}
associated with the first eigenvalue
\mu_{1}>0
, i.e.,

\Delta_{\gamma}\psi_{1}-\mu^{*}\psi_{1}-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{2}f\check{\phi}^{-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}\psi_{1}=-\mu_{1}\psi_{1}.

Using Proposition
11.1
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})[\psi_{1}]

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\gamma}\psi_{1}-\mu^{*}\psi_{1}-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{2}f\check{\phi}^{-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}\psi_{1}+c^{i}\nabla_{i}\psi_{1}+c\psi_{1}

\displaystyle=-\mu_{1}\psi_{1}+c^{i}\nabla_{i}\psi_{1}+c\psi_{1}.

From derived apriori estimates and constructed lower barrier for
\check{\phi}
, we have
|\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla^{2}_{\gamma}\check{\phi}|+|\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla_{\gamma}\check{\phi}|^{2}+1
is independent of
u,\underline{u}
and it satisfies

|c|+|c^{i}|\leq Ca^{-\frac{1}{3}}\Omega^{\tilde{\delta}}(|\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla^{2}_{\gamma}\check{\phi}|+|\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla_{\gamma}\check{\phi}|^{2}+1).

With
\Omega
being sufficiently small depending on
u,\underline{u}
in the interior region,
we have

|c^{i}|+|c|\ll 1
. This implies

\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})[\psi_{1}]<0
on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.
Employing
the calculation in the proof of
Theorem 2.11 in
[
26
]
, for any
w\in C^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
,
we consider an elliptic equation for
\frac{w}{\psi_{1}}
. For notional simplicity, let us rewrite the elliptic operator
\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})
as

\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})[w]=a_{ij}D_{ij}w+b_{i}D_{i}w+cw

with
a_{ij},b_{i},c
being functions. We then calculate and directly check that
\frac{w}{\psi_{1}}
satisfies

a_{ij}D_{ij}\frac{w}{\psi_{1}}+(b_{i}+\frac{2}{\psi_{1}}a_{ij}D_{j}\psi_{1})D_{i}\frac{w}{\psi_{1}}+\frac{\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})[\psi_{1}]}{\psi_{1}}\frac{w}{\psi_{1}}=\frac{\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})[w]}{\psi_{1}}.

(11.9)

Since
\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})[\psi_{1}]<0
and
\psi_{1}>0
, the above elliptic equation is invertible. For fixed
\psi_{1}>0
, giving
\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})[w]/\psi_{1}
, we can solve for the unique solution
\frac{w}{\psi_{1}}
. This further implies that
\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi}):C^{2,\beta}(\mathbb{S}^{2})\rightarrow C^{{0},\beta}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
is invertible and it holds

\begin{split}|\frac{w}{\psi_{1}}|_{C^{2,\beta}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\leq C|\frac{\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})[w]}{\psi_{1}}|_{C^{0,\beta}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad|w|_{C^{2,\beta}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\leq C|\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})[w]|_{C^{0,\beta}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\end{split}

(11.10)

with some positive constant
C
independent of
w
and depending on function
\check{\phi}
and its associated eigenfunction
\psi_{1}
.

∎

In the next lemma, we eliminate such dependence
on
\check{\phi}
.

Lemma 11.4
.

Assume
f
and
\check{\phi}
to be given nonnegative functions on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
with (
1.4
) satisfied and

|\check{\phi}|_{C^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\leq K
with
K
obeying (
9.14
). Then, within the hyperbolic region, for any
w\in C^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
, we have that

\max_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|w|\leq C\max_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})[w]|.

(11.11)

Here the positive constant
C
depends only on

m
and
\epsilon
in (
1.4
) and
K
,
independent of
\check{\phi}
and
\underline{u}\in(0,\delta]
.

Proof.

We take a smooth nonnegative function
f_{0}=f_{0}(\omega)
on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
satisfying
f_{0}\geq m/2
on
B_{p}(\epsilon)
and
f(\cdot,\underline{u})\geq f_{0}
on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
for all
\underline{u}\in(0,\delta]
.
With the standard spherical metric
\gamma_{0}
on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, we first consider

L_{*}w=\Delta_{\gamma_{0}}w{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\mu^{*}w-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{2}f_{0}w}.

Employing Lemma
11.2
to
L_{*}
, we can find a positive
eigenfunction
\psi_{1}
associated with its first eigenvalue
\mu_{1}>0
, i.e.,

\Delta_{\gamma_{0}}\psi_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\mu^{*}\psi_{1}-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{2}f\psi_{1}}=-\mu_{1}\psi_{1}.

Here
\mu_{1}
and
\psi_{1}
depend only on
m
,
\epsilon
and are independent of
\underline{u}\in(0,\delta]
.
With the fact
0<\check{\phi}\leq 1
,

f\geq f_{0}
, applying Lemma
9.2
, and Proposition
11.1
, we then get

\displaystyle\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\check{\phi}})[\psi_{1}]

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\gamma_{0}}\psi_{1}-\mu^{*}\psi_{1}-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{2}f\check{\phi}^{-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}\psi_{1}+\Delta_{\gamma}\psi_{1}-\Delta_{\gamma_{0}}\psi_{1}+c^{i}\nabla_{i}\psi_{1}+c\psi_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{\gamma_{0}}\psi_{1}-\mu^{*}\psi_{1}-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{2}f_{0}\psi_{1}+\Delta_{\gamma}\psi_{1}-\Delta_{\gamma_{0}}\psi_{1}+c^{i}\nabla_{i}\psi_{1}+c\psi_{1}

\displaystyle=-\mu_{1}\psi_{1}+\Delta_{\gamma}\psi_{1}-\Delta_{\gamma_{0}}\psi_{1}+c^{i}\nabla_{i}\psi_{1}+c\psi_{1}\leq-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c(m,\epsilon,K)}<0.

For the last inequality, we choose
a
being suitably large in the exterior and
\Omega
being sufficiently small in the interior.
We then repeat the proof of Lemma
11.3
with this
\psi_{1}
, which is independent of
\check{\phi}
.
And we prove the current lemma. ∎

In below we further prove that the equation
\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})=0
admits a unique solution
\check{\phi}
satisfying

|\check{\phi}|_{C^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\leq K
.

Proposition 11.5
.

Set
f
and
\check{\phi}_{1},\check{\phi}_{2}
to be given nonnegative functions on
\mathbb{S}^{2}

with (
1.4
) satisfied. Require

|\check{\phi}_{1}|_{C^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\leq K
and
|\check{\phi}_{2}|_{C^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\leq K
with
K
obeying (
9.14
).
If
\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi}_{1})=\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi}_{2})
,
then it holds that
\check{\phi}_{1}\equiv\check{\phi}_{2}
for all
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

Proof.

For the given
\check{\phi}_{1}
and
\check{\phi}_{2}
, we define the below
\mathcal{L}
and view it as a linear operator of
\check{\phi}_{1}-\check{\phi}_{2}
.

\mathcal{L}(\check{\phi}_{1}-\check{\phi}_{2}):=\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi}_{1})-\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi}_{2})=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(t\check{\phi}_{1}+(1-t)\check{\phi}_{2})[\check{\phi}_{1}-\check{\phi}_{2}]dt.

The same reasoning as for Lemma 5.5
of
[
6
]
yields the desired result.

Here operator
\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(t\check{\phi}_{1}+(1-t)\check{\phi}_{2})dt
possesses a similar structure to the linearized operator
\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi})
in Proposition
11.1
. The corresponding Lemmas
11.2
-
11.4
can be obtained in the same manner. By the assumption, we first use
\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi}_{1})\geq\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi}_{2})
and it gives
\mathcal{L}(\check{\phi}_{1}-\check{\phi}_{2})\geq 0
. With the same proof as in Lemma
11.3
, for the operator
\mathcal{L}
, applying Theorem 2.11 in
[
26
]
, the strong maximum principle holds here. Hence it holds either
\check{\phi}_{1}<\check{\phi}_{2}
or
\check{\phi}_{1}\equiv\check{\phi}_{2}
on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
. We then use
\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi}_{2})\geq\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi}_{1})
. Repeating the argument, we now have either
\check{\phi}_{2}<\check{\phi}_{1}
or
\check{\phi}_{1}\equiv\check{\phi}_{2}
on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
. Combining these together, we have
proved
the desired uniqueness result.
∎

As a corollary of the above lemma, we also prove that

Theorem 11.6
.

Take
\underline{u}
to be a fixed constant in
(0,\delta]
. Let
f
be a given smooth function on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
,
satisfying (
Theorem
) and (
1.4
),
and
\underline{\check{\phi}}
and
\overline{\check{\phi}}

be constructed as in Section
9
. Then, there exists a unique smooth solution
{\check{\phi}}=\check{\phi}(\omega,\underline{u})
of
\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi},\underline{u})=0
, satisfying

\underline{\check{\phi}}<\check{\phi}(\cdot,\underline{u})<\overline{\check{\phi}}
on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

Proof.

Here, for any constant
\lambda\in[0,1]
and
any
C^{2}
-function
\check{\phi}
on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, we set

\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}(\check{\phi})=\Delta_{\gamma}\check{\phi}-\mu^{*}\check{\phi}+\frac{\mu^{*}}{2}\big[\lambda f+(1-\lambda)]\check{\phi}^{1-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}+\lambda(I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}+I_{5}),

where
I_{i}
, with
2\leq i\leq 5
, are defined in (
11.5
).
With the same line of reasoning as for the detailed proof of Theorem 6.1 in
[
6
]
, by applying the method of continuity for parameter
\lambda\in[0,1]
, together with the utilization of the invertibility of
\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}_{\lambda}(\check{\phi})
, we conclude the existence. The uniqueness follows from Proposition
11.5
.

∎

12.
The Anisotropic Apparent Horizon

Considering
\underline{u}
as a parameter, in this section we show that our constructed MOTSs form a smooth apparent horizon. Viewing the solution
\check{\phi}
in Theorem
11.6
as a function of
\omega
and
\underline{u}
, we have

Theorem 12.1
.

With the assumptions in Theorem
11.6
,
for each
\underline{u}\in(0,\delta]
, setting
\check{\phi}=\check{\phi}(\omega,\underline{u})
to be the solution
to
\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi},\underline{u})=0
as in Theorem
11.6
,
then it holds that the function
\check{\phi}=\check{\phi}(\omega,\underline{u})
is smooth with respect to both variables
(\omega,\underline{u})\in\mathbb{S}^{2}\times(0,\delta]
.

Proof.

We employ the method of continuity again as in Section
11
. This time we take
\underline{u}
as a new parameter. Given
\check{\phi}(\omega,\underline{u})
being the constructed unique solution to
\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi},\underline{u})=0
, we then apply the implicit function theorem to obtain a smooth solution
\check{\phi}(\omega,\underline{u}^{\prime})
to
\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi},\underline{u}^{\prime})=0
with
\underline{u}^{\prime}
close to
\underline{u}
. The key is the invertibility of
\partial_{\check{\phi}}\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi},\underline{u}):C^{2,\beta}(\mathbb{S}^{2})\rightarrow C^{0,\beta}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
, which was proved in Lemma
11.3
. Hence,
\check{\phi}(\omega,\underline{u})
is smooth with respect to both
\omega
and
\underline{u}
variables.
∎

Applying
a null comparison principle and an upcoming theorem in
[
7
]
by the author and He
, with double null foliations we show that the apparent horizon formed in gravitational collapse is always either null or spacelike.

In this section, we specify a condition of
f
to guarantee the spacelikeness of our constructed apparent horizon
\{u=-(\underline{u}a)^{1-\mu^{*}}\check{\phi}^{\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}}\}
. To do so, we derive the equation for
\partial_{\underline{u}}\check{\phi}

and to obtain its estimate.

Proposition 12.2
.

Let
\check{\phi}=\check{\phi}(\omega,\underline{u})
be the solution
to
\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi},\underline{u})=0
as in Theorem
11.6
. For each
\underline{u}\in(0,\delta]
,
\partial_{\underline{u}}\check{\phi}
obeys

\Delta_{\gamma}(\partial_{\underline{u}}\check{\phi})-\mu^{*}\partial_{\underline{u}}\check{\phi}-\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{2}f(\omega,\underline{u})\check{\phi}^{-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}\partial_{\underline{u}}\check{\phi}+c^{i}\nabla_{i}(\partial_{\underline{u}}\check{\phi})+c\partial_{\underline{u}}\check{\phi}=\frac{1}{\underline{u}}h+\frac{\mu^{*}}{2}\partial_{\underline{u}}f\check{\phi}^{1-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}.

(12.1)

Here
c=c(\omega,\check{\phi},\underline{u})
,
c^{i}=c^{i}(\omega,\check{\phi},\underline{u})
and
h=h(\omega,\check{\phi},\underline{u})

are functions satisfying

|c|+|c^{i}|+|h|\leq Ca^{-\frac{1}{3}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{\tilde{\delta}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\check{\phi}^{-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(|\nabla^{2}_{\gamma}\check{\phi}|+|\nabla_{\gamma}\check{\phi}|^{2}+1)}.

(12.2)

Proof.

The main part of this proof is similar to the corresponding one in
[
6
]
and hence is omitted. Only noting that, in the estimates for here we employ

\alpha_{1}:=-[\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}_{g}\underline{\chi}+\frac{1}{R}]-4[\Omega\underline{\omega}-(\Omega\underline{\omega})_{0}],

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}:=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}[(\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\mbox{tr}\chi+|\hat{\chi}|_{g}^{2}+\partial_{4}\phi\partial_{4}\phi)(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)]d\underline{u}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\frac{\Omega^{-2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)}{|u|^{2}}\bigg(|\Omega\hat{\chi}|_{g}^{2}(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)+[\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)-\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)]^{2}\bigg)d\underline{u}^{\prime}.

The desired estimates for
\partial_{u}\alpha_{1}
and
\partial_{u}\alpha_{2}
were already obtained in Proposition
11.1
. To bound
\partial_{\underline{u}}\alpha_{1}
and
\partial_{\underline{u}}\alpha_{2}
, we utilize the explicit formulas for
\partial_{\underline{u}}[\frac{1}{2}\Omega\mbox{tr}_{g}\underline{\chi}+\frac{1}{R}]
and
\partial_{\underline{u}}(\Omega\underline{\omega})
, the estimates for
\partial_{\underline{u}}\alpha_{1}
can be obtained straightforwardly. And for
\partial_{\underline{u}}\alpha_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\underline{u}}\alpha_{2}(u,\underline{u},\omega)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\times(\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\mbox{tr}\chi+|\hat{\chi}|_{g}^{2}+\partial_{4}\phi\partial_{4}\phi)(u,\underline{u},\omega)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\times\frac{\Omega^{-2}(u,\underline{u},\omega)}{|u|^{2}}\bigg(|\Omega\hat{\chi}|_{g}^{2}(-1,\underline{u},\omega)+[\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)-\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)]^{2}\bigg).

The desired estimate for above expression can be got in the same fashion as for (
11.8
).
∎

Proceeding similarly to the argument for Corollary 6.4 in
[
6
]
, we then obtain

Corollary 12.3
.

For each
\underline{u}\in(0,\delta]
, let
\check{\phi}=\check{\phi}(\omega,\underline{u})
be the solution
to
\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi},\underline{u})=0
. Under the assumptions in Theorem
11.6
, it holds

\max_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\partial_{\underline{u}}\check{\phi}|\leq C\big[\underline{u}^{-1}a^{-\frac{1}{3}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{\tilde{\delta}}}\max_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\check{\phi}|+|\nabla_{\gamma}\check{\phi}|+|\nabla^{2}_{ij}\check{\phi}|+|\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla_{\gamma}\check{\phi}\nabla_{\gamma}\check{\phi}|})+\max_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\partial_{\underline{u}}f{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\check{\phi}^{1-\frac{1}{\mu^{*}}}}|\big],

(12.3)

where
C
is a positive constant independent of
a
,
\underline{u}
and
\check{\phi}
.

This further implies

Proposition 12.4
.

For each
\underline{u}\in(0,\delta]
, set
\check{\phi}=\check{\phi}(\omega,\underline{u})
to be the solution
of
\mathcal{S}(\check{\phi},\underline{u})=0
. Under the assumptions in Theorem
11.6
, if we further require

|\underline{u}\partial_{\underline{u}}f(\omega,\underline{u})|\leq a^{-\frac{1}{3}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Omega^{\tilde{\delta}}(u_{1})},

(12.4)

the apparent horizon
\{(\omega,u,\underline{u})|\,u=-(\underline{u}a)^{1-\mu^{*}}\check{\phi}^{\lambda}(\omega,\underline{u})\}
is spacelike.

Proof.

Using
R=(\underline{u}a)^{1-\mu^{*}}\check{\phi}^{\lambda}(\omega,\underline{u})
and
\lambda=\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}
, we have
\partial_{\underline{u}}R=\underline{u}^{-1}R[(1-\mu^{*})+\lambda\underline{u}\check{\phi}^{-1}\partial_{\underline{u}}\check{\phi}]
.
Applying Corollary
12.3
, if
|\underline{u}\partial_{\underline{u}}f(\omega,\underline{u})|\leq a^{-\frac{1}{3}}\Omega^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tilde{\delta}}}
, then we get
\partial_{\underline{u}}R\geq\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{2}\underline{u}^{-1}R
.
Assume
g^{\prime}
is the induced metric to the apparent horizon

u=-R(\omega,\underline{u})
. Rewrite
\omega\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
as
\omega=(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
. As shown in
[
3
]
, then it holds

\displaystyle g^{\prime}_{\theta_{i}\theta_{j}}=g_{\theta_{i}\theta_{j}},\quad g^{\prime}_{\theta_{i}\underline{u}}=2\partial_{\theta_{i}}R,\quad g^{\prime}_{\underline{u}\,\underline{u}}=4\partial_{\underline{u}}R.

Suppose
X=\lambda_{1}\partial_{\theta_{1}}+\lambda_{2}\partial_{\theta_{2}}+\lambda_{3}\partial_{\underline{u}}
to be an arbitrary
nonzero tangent vector along the apparent horizon with constants
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda_{3}
. It holds

\displaystyle g^{\prime}(X,X)=\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}g_{ij}+4\lambda_{i}\lambda_{3}\partial_{i}R+4\lambda_{3}^{2}\partial_{\underline{u}}R,

where
i,j=1,2
are summed over.
The case
\lambda_{3}=0
implies
g^{\prime}(X,X)=\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}g_{ij}>0
. For the case
\lambda_{3}\neq 0
, using
g_{ij}=R^{2}\gamma_{ij}
and
\partial_{i}R=\lambda R\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}
, we get

\begin{split}g^{\prime}(X,X)=&\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}g_{ij}+4\lambda_{i}\lambda_{3}\partial_{i}R+4\lambda_{3}^{2}\partial_{\underline{u}}R\\
\geq&R^{2}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}\gamma_{ij}+4\lambda_{i}\lambda_{3}\lambda R\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla_{i}\check{\phi}+4\lambda_{3}^{2}\cdot\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{2}\underline{u}^{-1}R\\
\geq&2\lambda_{3}^{2}(1-\mu^{*})\underline{u}^{-1}R-4\lambda_{3}^{2}\lambda^{2}(\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla_{i}\check{\phi})^{2}\\
>&2\lambda_{3}^{2}(1-\mu^{*})\underline{u}^{-\mu^{*}}(am\epsilon^{\tau+2})^{1-\mu^{*}}-4\lambda_{3}\lambda^{2}(\check{\phi}^{-1}\nabla_{i}\check{\phi})^{2}.\end{split}

(12.5)

For the last inequality, we use
\bar{\widetilde{\phi}}_{max}=-\log(m\epsilon^{\tau+2})+C(\tau)
in (
9.12
) and hence

R\geq(\underline{u}a\cdot m\epsilon^{\tau+2})^{1-\mu^{*}}\mbox{ with }\tau>2.2\mbox{ fixed}.

At the same time, by (
9.11
) we have
u=-(\frac{2}{3}\underline{u}a)^{1-\mu^{*}}
is an untrapped surface. For the constructed MOTS locating at
u=-R=-(\underline{u}a)^{1-\mu^{*}}\check{\phi}^{\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}}
, it thus holds

\check{\phi}^{\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}}\geq(\frac{2}{3})^{1-\mu^{*}}
. That is
\check{\phi}\geq(\frac{2}{3})^{\mu^{*}}
.
Therefore, back to (
12.5
), when
\underline{u}
is sufficiently small, we have
g^{\prime}(X,X)>0
. This renders our constructed apparent horizon spacelike
and hence a dynamical horizon.
∎

13.
The Instability Theorems

In this section, we prove nonlinear instability theorems with Christodoulou’s naked-singularity initial data prescribed along
\underline{u}=0
.
17
17

17

For perturbed initial data, analogous instability theorems can be proved correspondingly.

13.1.
Instability Subject to Perturbations with Finite BV Norms

We first recall equation (
5.1
)

(\Omega e_{3})(\Omega e_{4}\phi)+\frac{1}{2}{\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}}\Omega e_{4}\phi=\Omega^{2}\Delta_{g}\phi-\frac{1}{2}{\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi}\Omega e_{3}\phi{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+2\Omega^{2}\eta^{A}e_{A}\phi}.

With
e_{3}=\Omega^{-1}{\partial}/{\partial u}
, this equation is equivalent to

\begin{split}\frac{\partial}{\partial u}(u\Omega e_{4}\phi)+\frac{1}{2}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{|u|})u\Omega e_{4}\phi=u\Omega^{2}\Delta_{g}\phi-\frac{1}{2}u\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot\Omega e_{3}\phi+2u\Omega^{2}\eta^{A}e_{A}\phi.\end{split}

Via integration, we hence obtain

\begin{split}u\Omega e_{4}\phi(u,\underline{u},\omega)=&u\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)+\int_{-1}^{u}-\frac{1}{2}u^{\prime}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot\Omega e_{3}\phi(u^{\prime},\underline{u},\omega)du^{\prime}\\
&+\int_{-1}^{u}[-\frac{1}{2}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{|u|})\cdot u\Omega e_{4}\phi+u\Omega^{2}\Delta_{g}\phi+2u\Omega^{2}\eta^{A}e_{A}\phi](u^{\prime},\underline{u},\omega)du^{\prime}.\end{split}

(13.1)

For the first two terms on the right of (
13.1
), we can rewrite them as

\begin{split}&u\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)+\int_{-1}^{u}-\frac{1}{2}u^{\prime}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot\Omega e_{3}\phi(u^{\prime},\underline{u},\omega)du^{\prime}\\
=&u\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)-u\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)+\underline{u\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{-1}^{u}u^{\prime}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi\cdot[\Omega e_{3}\phi-(\Omega e_{3}\phi)_{0}](u^{\prime},\underline{u},\omega)du^{\prime}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\int_{-1}^{u}u^{\prime}[\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi-(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0}]\cdot(\Omega e_{3}\phi)_{0}(u^{\prime},\underline{u},\omega)du^{\prime}\underline{-\frac{1}{2}\int_{-1}^{u}u^{\prime}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0}\cdot(\Omega e_{3}\phi)_{0}(u^{\prime},\underline{u},\omega)du^{\prime}}.\end{split}

Employing (
13.1
), with Christodoulou’s initial data along
\underline{u}=0
we have

u\Omega e_{4}\phi(u,0,\omega)=u\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)+\int_{-1}^{u}-\frac{1}{2}u^{\prime}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0}\cdot(\Omega e_{3}\phi)_{0}(u^{\prime},0,\omega)du^{\prime}.

Hence, the underlined two terms can be replaced by
u\Omega e_{4}\phi(u,0,\omega)
. Back to (
13.1
) again, we now obtain

\begin{split}&u\Omega e_{4}\phi(u,\underline{u},\omega)-[u\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)-u\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)]\\
=&u\Omega e_{4}\phi(u,0,\omega)-\frac{1}{2}\int_{-1}^{u}u^{\prime}\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi[\Omega e_{3}\phi-(\Omega e_{3}\phi)_{0}](u^{\prime},\underline{u},\omega)du^{\prime}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\int_{-1}^{u}u^{\prime}[\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi-(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0}]\cdot(\Omega e_{3}\phi)_{0}(u^{\prime},\underline{u},\omega)du^{\prime}\\
&+\int_{-1}^{u}[-\frac{1}{2}(\Omega\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}+\frac{2}{|u|})\cdot u\Omega e_{4}\phi+u\Omega^{2}\Delta_{g}\phi+2u\Omega^{2}\eta^{A}e_{A}\phi](u^{\prime},\underline{u},\omega)du^{\prime}.\end{split}

Utilizing the fact

u\Omega e_{4}\phi(u,0,\omega)=-\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}}\cdot\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{4}\cdot|u|^{\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}}

and the hyperbolic estimates derived in previous sections, we get

|u\Omega e_{4}\phi(u,\underline{u},\omega)-[\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)-\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)]|\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|}+|u|^{\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}}.

(13.2)

Now we consider the key equation that drives the trapped surface formation

\Omega\nabla_{4}(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi-(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi)_{0})=-|\hat{\chi}|^{2}-(\nabla_{4}\phi)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}(\mbox{tr}\chi)^{2}.

With this equation, we have

\begin{split}&\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi(u,\underline{u},\omega)-(\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi)(u,0,\omega)\\
=&\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}-|\hat{\chi}|^{2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}-|\nabla_{4}\phi|^{2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}-\frac{1}{2}(\mbox{tr}\chi)^{2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}.\end{split}

(13.3)

We then find the main terms on the right. By hyperbolic estimates, we first have

|\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}-|\hat{\chi}|^{2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}-\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}-\frac{|\Omega\hat{\chi}|^{2}(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}d\underline{u}^{\prime}|\leq\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\cdot\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}.

Using (
13.2
), we also get

\begin{split}&|\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}-|\nabla_{4}\phi|^{2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}[\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)-\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)]^{2}du^{\prime}|\\
\leq&\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{|u|^{2}}\cdot(\frac{\underline{u}^{\prime}a}{|u|}+|u|^{\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}})\cdot a^{\frac{1}{2}}d\underline{u}^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\cdot(\frac{\underline{u}^{2}a^{\frac{3}{2}}}{|u|}+|u|^{\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}}\cdot\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}})\\
\leq&\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\cdot(\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}+|u|^{\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}}a^{-\frac{1}{2}}).\end{split}

For the last term, via the obtained estimate in Proposition
4.3

|\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi-\frac{2(1-\mu^{*})\Omega^{2}}{|u|}|\lesssim\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|^{2}},

we deduce

\begin{split}|\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}-\frac{1}{2}(\mbox{tr}\chi)^{2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}|\lesssim&\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\cdot(\Omega\mbox{tr}\chi)^{2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}\\
\leq&\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}(\frac{2(1-\mu^{*})\Omega^{2}}{|u|}+\frac{\underline{u}^{\prime}a}{|u|^{2}})^{2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}\\
\lesssim&\frac{\underline{u}}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\Omega^{4}(u,\underline{u},\omega)+\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\cdot\frac{\underline{u}^{2}a}{|u|^{2}}(u,\underline{u},\omega).\end{split}

Note that the MOTS forms in the interior region, where
\Omega(u,\underline{u},\omega)\lesssim\Omega(u,0)\leq\Omega(u_{1},0)\ll 1
. With these estimates, we spot the main contribution on the right of (
13.3
), that is

-\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\frac{|\Omega\hat{\chi}|^{2}(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}d\underline{u}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}[\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)-\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)]^{2}du^{\prime}.

We then define
f(\omega,\underline{u})
via requiring

\begin{split}&\frac{\underline{u}af(\omega,\underline{u})}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}(u,0)}\cdot(1-\mu^{*})\\
:=&\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\frac{|\Omega\hat{\chi}|^{2}(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)+[\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)-\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)]^{2}}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}(u,0)}d\underline{u}^{\prime}.\end{split}

(13.4)

With this defined
f(\omega,\underline{u})
, combining all the previous arguments above, we arrive at our first main theorem

Theorem 13.1
.

Consider the characteristic initial value problem for the Einstein-scalar field system (
1.1
). Assigning Christodoulou’s naked-singularity initial data in
[
18
]
along
\underline{u}=0
with
-1\leq u\leq 0
and prescribing perturbed initial data along

satisfying

\sum_{i\leq 5,j\leq 3}\underline{u}^{j}a^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|(\partial_{\underline{u}})^{j}\nabla^{i}\hat{\chi}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{-1,\underline{u}})}+\underline{u}^{j}a^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|(\partial_{\underline{u}})^{j}\nabla^{i}\nabla_{4}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}_{\underline{u}}L^{2}(S_{-1,\underline{u}})}\leq B,

(13.5)

then for each
B
there exist a sufficient small
\delta=\delta(B)
and constant
a=a(B)
18
18

18

In particular, we can choose
a=1
.
, and the Einstein-scalar field system admits a unique regular solution in the spacetime region

, where

and

.

Moreover, if we further assume

\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}|\hat{\chi}|^{2}(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)+[\nabla_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)-\nabla_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)]^{2}d\underline{u}^{\prime}=f(\omega,\underline{u})\underline{u}a

(13.6)

with

obeying

for a point

with

, and the collection of MOTS

Set
a=1
, we then get Theorem
1.1
.

Note that for
\underline{u}<0
we require our spacetime to coincide with Christodoulou’s naked-singularity solution in
[
18
]
. Along
u=-1
we consider the possible extensions of
\hat{\chi}(-1,\underline{u},\omega),\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)
for
\underline{u}>0
. Via the next theorem, we can see that Christodoulou’s naked-singularity solution is associated with nonlinear instability of high co-dimensions:

Theorem 13.2
.

Consider the space of prescribed initial data for
\hat{\chi}(-1,\underline{u},\omega)
and
\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)
along
u=-1
with
\underline{u}>0
. With
|\hat{\chi}|(-1,0,\omega)=0,\,\,\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi(-1,0,\omega)=\sqrt{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{\mu^{*}}}\cdot\frac{1-\mu^{*}}{4}
corresponding to their values of Christodoulou’s naked-singularity solution in
[
18
]
, then for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, we can find smooth functions
f_{1},f_{2},...,f_{k}
and
g_{1},g_{2},...,g_{k}
with variables
\underline{u}
and
\omega
, such that the below prescribed initial data for
|\hat{\chi}|
and
\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi
with
\underline{u}>0

\begin{split}&\bigg(|\hat{\chi}|(-1,\underline{u},\omega),\quad\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)\bigg)\\
=&\bigg(0+\lambda_{1}f_{1}+\lambda_{2}f_{2}+\cdot\cdot\cdot+\lambda_{k}f_{k},\quad\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi(-1,0,\omega)+\lambda_{1}^{\prime}g_{1}+\lambda_{2}^{\prime}g_{2}+\cdot\cdot\cdot+\lambda_{k}^{\prime}g_{k}\bigg)\end{split}

would lead to the trapped surface and the apparent horizon formation, when

\sum_{1}^{k}\lambda_{i}^{2}+\sum_{1}^{k}{\lambda_{i}^{\prime}}^{2}\neq 0\quad\mbox{ with }\quad\lambda_{k},\lambda_{k}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}.

Moreover, if it holds

\begin{split}&\bigg(0+\lambda_{1}f_{1}+\lambda_{2}f_{2}+\cdot\cdot\cdot+\lambda_{k}f_{k},\quad\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi(-1,0,\omega)+\lambda_{1}^{\prime}g_{1}+\lambda_{2}^{\prime}g_{2}+\cdot\cdot\cdot+\lambda_{k}^{\prime}g_{k}\bigg)\\
=&\bigg(0+\tilde{\lambda}_{1}f_{1}+\tilde{\lambda}_{2}f_{2}+\cdot\cdot\cdot+\tilde{\lambda}_{k}f_{k},\quad\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi(-1,0,\omega)+\tilde{\lambda}_{1}^{\prime}g_{1}+\tilde{\lambda}_{2}^{\prime}g_{2}+\cdot\cdot\cdot+\tilde{\lambda}_{k}^{\prime}g_{k}\bigg),\end{split}

then it renders

\lambda_{i}=\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\lambda_{i}^{\prime}=\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\quad\mbox{ with }\quad 1\leq i\leq k.

We may therefore say that, for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, Christodoulou’s naked-singularity solution in
[
18
]
has at least co-dimensional
2k
nonlinear instability subject to outgoing BV characteristic perturbations at
(-1,0,\omega)
.

Proof.

For any
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
and
0\leq\underline{u}\ll 1
, on the
2
-sphere
S_{-1,\underline{u}}
we first fix
2k
disjoint discs:
D_{1},D_{2},...,D_{k}
and
D_{1}^{\prime},D_{2}^{\prime},...,D_{k}^{\prime}
.
We prescribe smooth functions
f_{i}
being compactly supported in
D_{i}
and smooth functions
g_{i}
being compactly supported in
D_{i}^{\prime}
. For
1\leq i\leq k
, we further require

0\leq f_{i}\leq a^{\frac{1}{2}}\mbox{ on }\mathbb{S}^{2}\times(0,\delta],\quad f_{i}(\cdot,\underline{u})\geq m_{i}a^{\frac{1}{2}}\mbox{ on }B_{p_{i}}(\epsilon_{i})\subset D_{i},

0\leq g_{i}\leq a^{\frac{1}{2}}\mbox{ on }\mathbb{S}^{2}\times(0,\delta],\quad g_{i}(\cdot,\underline{u})\geq m_{i}^{\prime}a^{\frac{1}{2}}\mbox{ on }B_{p_{i}^{\prime}}(\epsilon_{i}^{\prime})\subset D_{i}^{\prime}

with points
p_{i}\in D_{i}\subset\mathbb{S}^{2}
,
p_{i}^{\prime}\in D_{i}^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{S}^{2}
and constants
m_{i},m_{i}^{\prime}\in(0,1)
,
\epsilon_{i},\epsilon_{i}^{\prime}\in(0,\pi/2)
. With these choices of initial data, conditions (
13.6
) and (
13.7
) in Theorem

are satisfied and the corresponding conclusions for trapped surface and apparent horizon formation hold. And if we have

\begin{split}&\bigg(0+\lambda_{1}f_{1}+\lambda_{2}f_{2}+\cdot\cdot\cdot+\lambda_{k}f_{k},\quad\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi(-1,0,\omega)+\lambda_{1}^{\prime}g_{1}+\lambda_{2}^{\prime}g_{2}+\cdot\cdot\cdot+\lambda_{k}^{\prime}g_{k}\bigg)\\
=&\bigg(0+\tilde{\lambda}_{1}f_{1}+\tilde{\lambda}_{2}f_{2}+\cdot\cdot\cdot+\tilde{\lambda}_{k}f_{k},\quad\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi(-1,0,\omega)+\tilde{\lambda}_{1}^{\prime}g_{1}+\tilde{\lambda}_{2}^{\prime}g_{2}+\cdot\cdot\cdot+\tilde{\lambda}_{k}^{\prime}g_{k}\bigg),\end{split}

since all the
f_{i}
and
g_{i}
with
1\leq i\leq k
are compacted supported in disjoint discs, it immediately implies

\lambda_{i}=\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\lambda_{i}^{\prime}=\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\quad\mbox{ with }\quad 1\leq i\leq k.

∎

We start from reconsidering

\begin{split}&\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi(u,\underline{u},\omega)-\frac{2(1-\mu^{*})}{|u|}\\
=&\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}-|\hat{\chi}|^{2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}-|\nabla_{4}\phi|^{2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}-\frac{1}{2}(\mbox{tr}\chi)^{2}(u,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)d\underline{u}^{\prime}.\end{split}

By the estimates derived above, we have

\begin{split}&|\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi(u,\underline{u},\omega)-\frac{2(1-\mu^{*})}{|u|}-\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\frac{|\Omega\hat{\chi}|^{2}(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)+[\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)-\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)]^{2}}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}(u,0,\omega)}d\underline{u}^{\prime}|\\
\leq&\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\cdot\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}+\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\cdot(\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|}+|u|^{\frac{\mu^{*}}{1-\mu^{*}}}a^{-\frac{1}{2}})+\frac{\underline{u}}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\Omega^{4}(u,\underline{u},\omega)+\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\cdot\frac{\underline{u}^{2}a}{|u|^{2}}(u,\underline{u},\omega).\end{split}

Together with
\Omega(u,0)=|u|^{\frac{\mu^{*}}{2(1-\mu^{*})}}
, this implies

\begin{split}&|\Omega^{-1}\mbox{tr}\chi(u,\underline{u},\omega)-\frac{2(1-\mu^{*})}{|u|}-\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\frac{|\Omega\hat{\chi}|^{2}(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)+[\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u}^{\prime},\omega)-\Omega e_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)]^{2}}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}(u,0,\omega)}d\underline{u}^{\prime}|\\
\leq&\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\cdot\Omega^{\frac{3}{2}}\cdot[\frac{\underline{u}a^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|u|\Omega^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\frac{\Omega^{\frac{1}{2}}}{a^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\frac{\Omega^{\frac{5}{2}}}{a}]\ll\frac{\underline{u}a}{|u|^{2}\Omega^{2}}\cdot\Omega^{\frac{3}{2}}.\end{split}

For the
f(\omega,\underline{u})
introduced in (

and

(13.8)

and

,

Proof.

. Note that we can set

to verify all the requirement of this theorem. The rest proof is the same as in Theorem

.

At the end of this section, we check the size of the initial perturbation. We first prove

Proposition 13.4
.

For
0\leq\underline{u}\leq\delta
, it holds

\|[\ln(\ln\frac{1}{\underline{u}})]^{-\frac{1}{4}}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}([0,\delta])}\leq\frac{C}{|\ln\frac{1}{\delta}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}

with
C
a uniform positive constant.

Proof.

Let
\hat{f}(\underline{u}):=[\ln(\ln\frac{1}{\underline{u}})]^{-\frac{1}{4}}
. Using the Sobolev-Slobodeckij space, we have

\begin{split}\|\hat{f}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}([0,\delta])}=&\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq x\leq\delta,\\
0\leq y\leq\delta\end{subarray}}\frac{|\hat{f}(x)-\hat{f}(y)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{2}}dxdy=2\iint_{0\leq x\leq y\leq\delta}\frac{|\hat{f}(x)-\hat{f}(y)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{2}}dxdy\\
=&2\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq x\leq y\leq\delta,\\
x\leq y\leq 2x\end{subarray}}\frac{|\hat{f}(x)-\hat{f}(y)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{2}}dxdy+2\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq x\leq y\leq\delta,\\
y>2x\end{subarray}}\frac{|\hat{f}(x)-\hat{f}(y)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{2}}dxdy\\
=&\hat{I}_{1}+\hat{I}_{2}.\end{split}

For
\hat{I}_{1}
, applying the intermediate value theorem, with
\theta
being a constant
\in[0,1]
, we get

\begin{split}\hat{I}=&2\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq x\leq y\leq\delta,\\
x\leq y\leq 2x\end{subarray}}\bigg(\frac{1}{4}[\ln(\ln\frac{1}{x+\theta(y-x)})]^{-\frac{5}{4}}\cdot\frac{1}{\ln\frac{1}{x+\theta(y-x)}}\cdot\frac{1}{x+\theta(y-x)}\bigg)^{2}dxdy\\
\lesssim&2\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq x\leq y\leq\delta,\\
x\leq y\leq 2x\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{(\ln\frac{1}{x})^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{y^{2}}dxdy\lesssim\int_{0}^{\delta}\frac{1}{(\ln\frac{1}{x})^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{x}dx\lesssim\frac{1}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}.\end{split}

To bound
\hat{I}_{2}
, we set
x:=e^{u}
and
y:=e^{v}
. The requirement
0<2x<y<\delta
implies

-\infty<u\leq-\ln\frac{1}{\delta}-\ln 2,\quad-\infty<v\leq-\ln\frac{1}{\delta}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad v-u>\ln 2>0.

Utilizing
(u,v)
and
(u,w)
coordinates with
w=v-u
, we then obtain

\begin{split}\hat{I}_{2}=&2\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq x\leq y\leq\delta,\\
y>2x\end{subarray}}\frac{|[\ln(\ln\frac{1}{y})]^{-\frac{1}{4}}-[\ln(\ln\frac{1}{x})]^{-\frac{1}{4}}|^{2}}{|y-x|^{2}}dxdy\\
\lesssim&2\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}-\infty<u,v\leq-\ln\frac{1}{\delta},\\
v-u\geq\ln 2\end{subarray}}e^{-2v}e^{u}e^{v}|[\ln(-v)]^{-\frac{1}{4}}-[\ln(-u)]^{-\frac{1}{4}}|^{2}dudv\\
\lesssim&2\int_{\ln 2}^{+\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{-\ln\frac{1}{\delta}-w}e^{-w}|[\ln(-u-w)]^{-\frac{1}{4}}-[\ln(-u)]^{-\frac{1}{4}}|^{2}dudw.\end{split}

The last line can be bounded as

\begin{split}\hat{I}_{2}\lesssim&2\int_{\ln 2}^{+\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{-\ln\frac{1}{\delta}-w}e^{-w}|\int_{-u}^{-u-w}-\frac{1}{4}(\ln r)^{-\frac{5}{4}}\cdot\frac{1}{r}dr|^{2}dudw\\
\lesssim&\int_{\ln 2}^{+\infty}\bigg(\int_{-\infty}^{-\ln\frac{1}{\delta}-w}e^{-w}\cdot\frac{w^{2}}{|u+w|^{2}}du\bigg)dw=\int_{\ln 2}^{+\infty}e^{-w}w^{2}\bigg(\int_{-\infty}^{-\ln\frac{1}{\delta}-w}\frac{1}{|u+w|^{2}}du\bigg)dw\\
=&\int_{\ln 2}^{+\infty}e^{-w}w^{2}\bigg(\int_{-\infty}^{-\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}\frac{1}{|\tilde{u}|^{2}}d\tilde{u}\bigg)dw\lesssim\frac{1}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}.\end{split}

∎

Back to Theorem
13.3
, along
H_{-1}^{[0,\delta]}
we could set the perturbation

|\hat{\chi}|(-1,\underline{u},\omega)+|\nabla_{4}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)-\nabla_{4}\phi(-1,0,\omega)|=[\ln(\ln\frac{1}{\underline{u}})]^{-\frac{1}{4}}\cdot\tilde{f}(\omega)^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}

(13.17)

with
\tilde{f}(\omega)
being a smooth function of
\omega
. Hence, to trigger the naked-singularity censoring, we only need the perturbation of
g
in scale-critical
\|\cdot\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}{(H_{-1}^{[0,\delta]}})}
norm, i.e.,

\mbox{the }\|\cdot\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}{(H_{-1}^{[0,\delta]}})}=\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}_{\omega}\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\underline{u}}([0,\delta])}+\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}_{\underline{u}}\dot{H}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\omega}(S_{-1,\underline{u}})}\mbox{ norm }

of the above perturbation in (
13.17
) to be of size
(\ln\frac{1}{\delta})^{-\frac{1}{2}}a^{\frac{1}{2}}\ll 1
. And as
\delta\rightarrow 0^{+}
, we allow the size of the scale-critical perturbation shrink to
0
.

Appendix A
Construction of Localized-In-Angle Initial Data

Along
u=-1
, in this appendix we construct the localized-in-angle initial data for
\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)
and
|\hat{\chi}|^{2}_{g}(-1,\underline{u},\omega)
with
\underline{u}\geq 0
. Since
\partial_{\underline{u}}\phi(-1,\underline{u},\omega)
is a scalar function, we can prescribe the desired localized-in-angle initial data as we need. Notice that
\hat{\chi}_{AB}(-1,\underline{u},\omega)
is a traceless
2
-form. To prescribe its characteristic initial data, we follow calculations in Chapter 2 of
[
21
]
and in Appendix C of
[
3
]
. With stereographic coordinates
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
, we have

g|_{S_{-1,\underline{u}}}=(\hat{\phi}|_{S_{-1,\underline{u}}})^{2}\hat{g}|_{S_{-1,\underline{u}}}

(A.1)

\mbox{with}\quad\hat{g}_{AB}(-1,\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=\frac{1}{1+\frac{1}{4}(\theta_{1}^{2}+\theta_{2}^{2})}\,m_{AB}(-1,\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})

and
m_{AB}
can be expressed as
m(-1,\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=\exp\Psi(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
. Here
\Psi
is a symmetric trace-free 2-dimensional matrix. Notice that as pointed out in Chapter 2 of
[
21
]
,
\Psi(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
is the free data we can prescribe along
u=-1
and
\hat{\phi}|_{S_{-1,\underline{u}}}
in (
A.1
) is determined by
\Psi(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
.
20
20

20

In the below, we will prescribe
\Psi(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
in the north polar chart. And
\Psi^{\prime}(\underline{u},\theta_{1}^{\prime},\theta_{2}^{\prime})
in the south polar chart can be obtained accordingly.

For
\sqrt{\theta^{2}_{1}+\theta^{2}_{2}}<20
we set

\Psi(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2}):=\sqrt{2}x(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\cdot a^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\Psi_{0}(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2}).

Here
\partial x(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})/\partial\underline{u}
is a free scalar function to be prescribed and we also choose

\Psi_{0}(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{bmatrix}\quad\mbox{for}\quad\underline{u}\geq 0.

With a similar calculation carried out in Appendix C of
[
3
]
, we deduce

\begin{split}\frac{\partial}{\partial\underline{u}}m(-1,\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=&\sqrt{2}a^{\frac{1}{2}}\begin{bmatrix}\exp\bigg(\sqrt{2}x(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\cdot a^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)&0\\
0&-\exp\bigg(-\sqrt{2}x(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\cdot a^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)\end{bmatrix}\\
&\times\frac{\partial x}{\partial\underline{u}}(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\end{split}

From Chapter 2 in
[
21
]
, we have

\hat{\chi}_{AB}=\frac{1}{2}\phi^{2}\frac{\partial\hat{g}}{\partial\underline{u}}=\frac{1}{2}\phi^{2}W^{2}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\frac{\partial}{\partial\underline{u}}m_{AB}(-1,\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\mbox{ and}

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}|\hat{\chi}|^{2}_{g}=&\frac{1}{8}(m^{-1})^{CA}(\frac{\partial m}{\partial\underline{u}})_{AB}(m^{-1})^{BD}(\frac{\partial m}{\partial\underline{u}})_{DC}.\end{split}

For our concrete construction, along
u=-1
it hence holds

\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}|\hat{\chi}|^{2}_{g}=&\frac{1}{8}\cdot\bigg(\sqrt{2}\cdot\frac{\partial x}{\partial\underline{u}}(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\cdot a^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)^{2}\cdot\mbox{tr}\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}=\frac{1}{2}a\cdot\bigg(\frac{\partial x}{\partial\underline{u}}(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\bigg)^{2}.\end{split}

(A.2)

With
\partial x(\underline{u},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})/\partial\underline{u}
as the freely prescribed scalar function on
S_{-1,\underline{u}}
, we therefore obtain the desired localized-in-angle initial data for
|\hat{\chi}|^{2}_{g}(-1,\underline{u},\omega)
.
